# Behavior-Specs-11 Coverage — full game functionality audit

Audit date: 2026-10-02. Covers `docs/behavior-specs-11/` (the 18 spec files plus `component-stats.tsv`). `behavior-specs-10-coverage.md` and the earlier
reports are kept alongside for history. **This report is the spec-11 audit of the code as it stands after the three spec-10 implementation passes**
(the per-file tables below are the spec-10 tables carried forward row by row; every spec-11 change is recorded in the rows as `**Spec-11:**` evidence,
new rows were appended at the end of each table, and the Impl/Test columns were re-verified against the current tree and test suite, which had
moved on since the spec-10 report - see "Where spec-11 overturns earlier work").

**What spec-11 is.** A corrective revision of spec-10 (whitespace-insensitive changed lines per file): ai-opponent-behavior 539, fleet-movement 215,
ship-design 198, client-ui-dialog-catalog 179, client-interface 106, turn-generation 99, race-designer 81, production 70, new-game 57, population 52,
combat 42, save-format 38, race-traits 37, victory 35, research 33. Unchanged: diplomacy-relations, dynamic-string-table, tutorial-system and
`component-stats.tsv`. The substance:

| Spec file | Substance |
|---|---|
| ai-opponent-behavior.md | **§7 (35/50/26/40-turn refresh thresholds, obsolete flag) retracted; the "resource-output discount" is retracted and replaced by the research share** (R minus p%, p per personality); the quick-dialog slot assignment, the race-naming procedure, the shared packet advisor (range table by doubled-driver flag, warp 13, "acted"), personality 0 and 1 planet passes (new), personality 5 pass (rewritten), personality 2/3/4 corrections, rendezvous, the coarse-defence value and the AR-visibility rule, section 15 try semantics, **section 15a starbase designer and section 17 starbase roles (entirely new)**, the alliances option |
| fleet-movement-scanning-cargo.md | **Orderable-warp limit retracted; minefield "+4 squared" and wormhole 0-99 detection retracted** (known/located masks, r/4 rule); the complete wormhole lifecycle (tier = base + age/5 - 2, jump vs 12-ly drift, pair counts by galaxy size, placement score); exact scanner ranges (penetration class, three non-scanner parts that scan); the Radiating Hydro-Ram hazard; fuel reach and messages 39/139; theft (colonists stealable); Transport pass equivalence and Load Optimal; **the 12-slot merge tally retracted**; Repeat Orders, step 10 normalisation, the pursuit re-lock, step 39; Stargate counting; detonation exact (owner-only exemption, no reveal, no wreckage); **Route and Transfer Fleet complete rules** |
| ship-design-and-components.md | **Opcode 0x1e, `FUN_10f0_0ff0` and the 36-byte table are the battle plans** (the slot-write story is withdrawn); frozen design prices; **penetrating ranges from the +54 class** (Robber Baron 120; Mega Poly Shell, MCM and Langston Shell scan); the ram-scoop hazard read |
| client-ui-dialog-catalog.md | Popup modes re-identified (3 fleet composition, 4 status strip, 5 environment axis, 7 three sentences, 8/12 mines and resources); Find in full; rename surfaces traced (31/12/31 chars, no filtering, empty clears); **the Ship Designer delete-in-use claim withdrawn**; the message group map, Goto kinds by subject word and the two-press "View"; report windows and text exports in full; **the Score window with Switch (scores / victory conditions / timeline)**; the Technology Browser |
| client-interface.md | **Planet views 0-5 exact** (bars with M = 5,000, value disc with v/t and an owner flag, population thresholds, mode 5), the view-option slot table and the start-up word 0x00E0; **the info-panel content modes retracted**; per-kind name builders (no "owned by"); the minefield selector retracted; planet route and packet controls; waypoint reorder settled (none); battle-plan deletion to the plan above |
| turn-generation-engine.md | **The 14 ordered pairs** that a port must keep (Nova's keys endorsed); the special-object pass order; the two ledgers; **Route is the mode-4 task** (Stargate handler retracted); 272 never produced, 78 lifecycle, **every player sees every Trader**; minefield corrections; overshoot at most 1; status bit 1 settled; **the complete mine-sweeping rule** (gatlings R = 4) |
| race-designer-ui-and-availability.md | Identity-stage limits (15/16) and rules; **the Random generator rewritten** (placeholder record + bit 30, rolled at universe creation, die of 25, 251 nudges); **complete preset records, Nucleotid corrected** (10/15/5); AR clears and disables the Germanium checkbox |
| production-queue.md | **Templates in full (slot 0 is the default; new empires empty; apply replaces only the auto-build part)**; packet merge test and allocation limits, no speed cap; **the production catalog 10l** (auto packets always, scanner item 27, AR exclusions); the exact terraform target rule and its second mode; **Packet Physics terraforming on arrival (10m)**; the 20% cut is the registration penalty |
| new-game-setup.md | **Simplified setup rewritten** (player-count table by difficulty, written defaults); **star count corrected** (Sparse subtracts), 12-ly fixed separation, **clumping weights inverted**, home-world placement by Starting Distance; **ordinary planets start with 0 surface minerals**; the second planet's own state; **"Reset to defaults" retracted**; the relation bytes are starting tech |
| population-growth.md | The home-world bit (one per race, never the second planet, survives capture) and remote-mining eligibility (non-AR refused at inhabited planets, AR only own planets); the summary popup body |
| combat-resolution.md | **Battle movement order exact** (per-ship weight, r 0-14 jitter, E ordering; the linear 20% rule superseded); the 0-14 value is not a firing tie-break; wreckage routine details; **the damage word outside battle** (merge, split, new ships, no damage in foreign reports); **bombing never triggers a tech gain**; the battle salvage dispatcher (rare-part table, research pool credit) |
| save-turn-file-format.md | Design caps and 31-char names have enforcement points (client + host reject); own-fleet damage words; **score visibility (own / game over / eliminated / Public Scores after counter 19; the host keeps no history)**; opcodes 0x1e = battle plans, 0x2e = default template |
| race-traits.md / victory-conditions.md / research-tech-tree.md | PP terraform-on-arrival now a rule; JOAT 20E/10E confirmed; second planet; `FUN_10f0_50e0` is wreckage (AI prices include BET); victory met bits regardless of enabled, the persistent winner mark, two races remaining, **the Score window is the Victory Conditions dialog**; **the research PRT exclusion retracted**, level 26 -> lowest field, the Technology Browser §7a |

**Method.** Fourteen analysis agents (one per changed spec file) read the full diffs, the spec-11 text and the *current code and tests* and returned
substantive changes, answered questions, overturned work, row-level impact, new gaps and what is implementable now. Their row impacts were applied to
the spec-10 tables below; where an analysis assumed a status from the spec-10 report that the tree had since outgrown (five turn-generation steps,
the Trader planet trade, the Simplified year gate, the `*CoverageTest`, `ComponentStatsConformanceTest` and `Nova.Avalonia.Tests` suites), the
columns were set to what the code and tests really are. Rows that spec-11 overturns and the code now contradicts show ⚠️ or ❌ with the contradiction
in the evidence. The open questions are maintained in `behavior-specs-10-questions.md` (answers and new questions appended there).

**Scope decisions (unchanged):**
- `tutorial-system.md` is **excluded** (unchanged by spec-11).
- `client-interface.md` and `client-ui-dialog-catalog.md` are included in full; the "UI differences for your review" section still separates
  real informational gaps from the port's deliberate dockable-panel shape.

**Spec-11 audit totals**, re-summed from the per-file tables with one convention (a row counts as applicable unless its Impl is N/A; full or partial
counts as implemented / tested); `dynamic-string-table.md` and `save-turn-file-format.md` keep their delta-maintained figures because their tables list
only the changed rows. The spec-10 report closed at 533/679 implemented and 375/690 tested (its summaries were delta-maintained, so the two totals are
not strictly comparable). Spec-11 moved the figures in both directions: 162 new rows were added (most ❌ or ⚠️ because spec-11 specifies behaviour for
the first time), 11 rows were retracted to N/A, 49 rows went from ✅ to ⚠️ or ❌ because the code now contradicts the spec, 9 stale rows went up
to ✅ (the five new turn steps, the Trader planet trade, the Simplified year gate, the terraform chooser, the wormhole "known" bit), 149 rows gained a
test tick from the `*CoverageTest`, `EngineRowsT3Test`, `ComponentStatsConformanceTest` and `Nova.Avalonia.Tests` suites that the spec-10 report had
not credited, and 32 rows lost one because their test now asserts the superseded reading. Row granularity still varies by file, so treat the
percentage as directional.

## Update (2026-10-04) — verification pass and follow-up fixes

A follow-up pass re-checked this report against the current tree (the per-file tables below are the spec-10 tables carried forward, so some rows had
outrun the code). It found several "gaps" already implemented, and landed the open, implementable items from "What's next" item 1 plus two client
items. The headline numbers above predate this update; the per-file tables below remain the detailed inventory.

**Verified already implemented in the tree (no change needed):**
- new-game §3 **ordinary planets start with 0 surface minerals** (#6): `StarMapInitialiser.GenerateStars` leaves every ordinary planet at zero;
  only planet 0 (the home-world template) and the Packet Physics / Interstellar Traveler second planet get tonnage.
- new-game §3 **star-count formula, fixed 12-ly sweep and clumping** (#7): `GameSettings.PresetStarCount` (Sparse subtracts, truncation),
  `StarMapGenerator.PlaceStarsByCount` (`PresetStarSeparation = 12`) and `RelaxStars`.
- new-game §3 **second planet's own state** (#8) and **Simplified setup / written defaults** (#9):
  `StarMapInitialiser` and `NewGameSetup.SimplifiedPlayerCountTable` / `ApplySimplifiedDefaults` / `ResetToDefaults`.
- **Expert +10% starting population** (#52): `Race.GetStartingPopulation(expertComputerPlayer)` adds the
  tenth for `PlayerSettings.AiSkill >= Race.ExpertAiSkill` (3), with a generated-home-world test.

**Landed (each with a unit test; main suite 3106/3106, `Nova.Avalonia.Tests` 105 passed / 1 skipped):**

| Report item | Fix |
|---|---|
| #4 gatling mine-sweep | fixed R = 4 for Mini Gun / Gatling Gun / Gatling Neutrino Cannon / Big Mutha Cannon, +1 on a starbase (commit `b0f4f81`) |
| #14 research PRT exclusion / level 26 | no race test anywhere in research; a target at level 26 with "same" moves to the lowest field (`b0f4f81`) |
| #16 theft | `TransportHandler.MayTakeFrom` no longer excludes colonists: every slot is stealable from a fleet or (full theft) a planet |
| #28 own-packet visibility | an own packet is not seen automatically (only within scanner range); a PP packet scanner only scans above warp 4 |
| #12 packet merge test | the merge test is the existing packet's mass **before** the merge, not the sum |
| #3 minefield routine | damage exactly equal to the armor survives; detonation exempts only the field owner's own Mini/Super Mine Layer hulls, reveals nothing and leaves no wreckage; a mine hit now removes the move's ram-scoop fuel generation (`Fleet.RemoveLastMoveFuelGeneration`) |
| #5 ram-scoop radiation | the colonist loss applies only to a fleet that moved under its own engines, and is at least 1 unit whenever any are aboard |
| #25 wreckage details | the grace mark goes only on the first receiving object; Scrap Fleet forces a new object; all-zero amounts invent 0-9 kT each; a bloodless battle makes no object |
| (not a spec row) golden test-isolation leak | `SimulationEnvironment` resets the process-wide `AllComponents` cache inside and after its private root, so a prior test's component load can no longer change the golden hashes |
| #47 battle-plan deletion | fleets on the deleted plan move to the plan **just above** it (`BattlePlanRules.PlanAbove`), not the first plan |
| #32 scanner data | Robber Baron Scanner penetrating is 120 (was 110); the three non-scanner parts scan per unit (Mega Poly Shell 80/40, Multi Contained Munition 150/75, Langston Shell 50/25) and feed the design's fourth-power scanner sum |
| #39 AI category 5 starbase advisor | the most-urgent mark of slots 1-3 now breaks ties by the earlier creation year, then the lower slot (`StarbaseAdvisors.MacintiMarks`); `PopulationPercent` returns 0 at zero capacity, so category 5's population upgrade rule cannot fire on an AR planet with no starbase |
| #34 AI shared packet advisor | the target range is now indexed by the doubled-driver flag (7,056 / 50,625 squared), tripled when a surface mineral exceeds 12,500 kT (surface stock, not projected); the gate uses the best driver warp (10+, no doubled +1); the destination speed is set to warp 13; and the advisor counts as acted once a target is drawn |
| #33 AI research share | `AiResearchShare.PercentFor` gives p per personality/turn (0 once all six techs are 24+); `DefaultPlanetAI.ProjectedResources()` deducts p × R / 100 unless the planet's leftover-only flag is set, and `Surplus`, `BuildShips`, `RunBomberDefence`, `RunTopUp` and the packet advisor read it. **The goldens were regenerated** (intended AI behavior change). |
| #35 AI personality 5 bulk packets (partial) | the entry tests now run before the 1-in-4 roll; a queued packet item abandons the step; the mineral is drawn uniformly and cycled Ironium → Boranium → Germanium; the target range is 91,204 squared (under 302 ly) with the source a candidate and 100,000 as the starting least; the speed field is set to warp 11. The nine-step planet pass itself is not ported. |
| #37 AI P4 chooser gating (partial) | personality 4's warship chooser now runs only at starbase planets outside the packet-hub slots (1, 3, 6, 8); a hub starbase builds no ships from the planet pass (`Cybertron_NothingAtAPacketHubStarbase_AndTheChooserDoesNotRun`). The other P2/P3/P4 corrections remain. |
| #1 minefield/wormhole detection | the complete minefield rule replaces the old (range + size + 4)² formula: a per-race permanent `Known` mask, full normal range for a known field, the penetrating range, r/4, and a ship's own inside-the-field test (planets have none, PP packets only their range); a mine hit and a sweep mark the field known. Wormhole detection uses the same full-range-if-located / r/4 / penetrating rule with **no roll** and a serialized `Located` mask. `IntelWriter` now carries only the fields seen this year. **Goldens regenerated** (the removed wormhole roll changed the RNG sequence). The located bit's clear-on-jump waits on the wormhole lifecycle (#2). |
| #2 wormhole lifecycle | each end now stores its base stability (uniform 0-2, drawn once) and age and *derives* the tier base + age/5 - 2 clamped to 0-6; generation draws the pair count by galaxy size (Tiny 0-2, Small 1-3, Medium 1-5, Large 3-6, Huge 4-8, none under No Random Events) and places every end with the new `WormholePlacement` squared-distance scorer. The yearly step rolls 0-99 below the tier: a jump clears every race's `Located` bit, resets the age (base kept) and re-places the end by the 100-draw rule; otherwise the age increments (wrapping at 1,024) and the end drifts up to 12 ly per axis, first score-0 candidate else lowest. The traversed mask and pairing never clear and no end ever disappears. **Goldens regenerated** (the placement/drift draws changed the RNG sequence). |
| #31 design caps / name limit (partial) | the design-name box truncates to 31 characters (`DesignCommand.MaxDesignNameLength`); `DesignCommand.IsAtDesignCap` (16 ship / 10 starbase) is available. Server-side enforcement of the cap or the name length is **deferred**: the AI design builder still adds a design each turn (SIM-2) and names designs over 31 characters (e.g. "Medium Freighter [colonizer] T2105"), so enforcing either changes every AI game and the goldens. |
| #15 remote-mining eligibility | `RemoteMiningStep` gates by PRT: a non-AR fleet is refused at any inhabited (owned) planet and an AR fleet mines only planets it owns; unowned planets still work for non-AR. The port has no Remote-Mining order object to cancel, so the refusal is simply "does not mine". |
| #17 Transfer Fleet | `TransferFleetTask.Transfer` now blocks only colonists (329), a computer recipient or one rating the giver Enemy (332) and a recipient with no free 16-ship/10-starbase design slot (330/331); minerals and fuel go with the ships, a copied design is flagged failed-legality, and each refusal posts to the giver (and 331 to the recipient). `TransferFleetStep` resolves the computer flag from `AllPlayers`. |
| #19 Repeat Orders and Patrol (partial) | a reached non-Patrol fleet-targeted waypoint is now recycled with its target (a pending Patrol intercept is not), recycling is skipped when the last waypoint stands on the reached point, and `PatrolStep.EfficientWarp` applies Patrol's free-speed preference (drop 1-3 warps to a free speed, both scoop engines excepted). The fuel 39/139 downgrade remains. |
| #13 Packet Physics terraforming on arrival | `PacketArrival.ApplyPacketTerraforming` (production-queue.md §10m): when the PP sender's packet is not fully caught, each uncaught mineral drives one axis (gravity/temperature/radiation) in turn; per 100 kT chunk a 0-199 roll below the chunk size is an ordinary hit and a 0-9 roll of 0 a permanent one. Permanent hits move the ORIGINAL value towards the sender's ideal (or the nearer end when immune); ordinary hits then move the CURRENT value towards the sender's own `TerraformProductionUnit.AxisTarget` (half when immune). Messages 305/307 to the sender and (per the spec's reimplementation note) 306/308 to the planet owner. **Goldens regenerated** (the terraform rolls changed the RNG sequence). |
| #26 turn order: bombardment before the second invasion pass | the post-battle stage now runs the bombing/artifact/research/Trader steps (keys 19-23) first, then `ColonizationResolver.ResolvePendingColonizations`, then Transfer Fleet and the remaining steps (key 24 on), matching pair 8 (behavior-specs-11/turn-generation-engine.md §1 step 23). `TurnOrderTest` probes the pending-landing ledger either side of the resolver. |
| #29 victory met bits, winner mark, two-races gate | `VictoryCheck` computes all seven condition-met bits whether or not each is enabled (`EmpireData.ConditionsMetMask` / `ScoreRecord.MetMask`) and counts only enabled ones; a qualifying race gets a persistent `Winner` mark once (spec bit 14, never cleared) only while at least two races remain; both fields round-trip through the save. `Scores` copies them onto every record. |
| #30 score visibility | a new `GameSettings.PublicPlayerScores` option (New Game checkbox) and `IntelWriter.VisibleScores`: a player receives race k's record only when k is its own, k is eliminated, the game is over (a winner mark exists), or Public Player Scores is on and the turn counter exceeds 19 (2420 onward). Other records stay private. **Goldens regenerated** (the new persisted fields changed the save hashes). |
| #3 message mapping (remaining) | the minefield transit and detonation recipient mapping in `CheckForMinefields` was already the spec's (197-200 to the fleet's owner, 201-204 to the field's owner; 351-353 to the fleet's owner and 354-356 to the field's owner); the two stale XML comments were swapped and are corrected. No behaviour change. |
| #22 battle movement order | `BattleEngine.MovementOrder` now gives each token its per-ship weight W (mass + cargo share, not multiplied by the stack count), draws its 0-14 random term, and sorts by E = W + 2(r-7)W/100 descending with array-order ties (`EffectiveMovementWeight`); the order is computed once per round and reused for the round's three steps, so they share r. The old linear `LighterMovesFirstChance`/20% parity swap and `TokenWeight` are gone. |
| #23 damage word (partial) | foreign fleet reports no longer leak damage: `FleetIntel.Update` copies no damage word and shows full armor on any report below `ScanLevel.Owned`; an owned report keeps its word. The full "the word is the only state" conversion (merge/exchange/new-ship join formulas) is still open. |
| #27 Trader mode-0 order (partial) | in the special-object pass mode 0, mass packets now take their full step (and may arrive and impact) **before** the Mystery Trader moves, both before fleets move (turn-generation-engine.md §1 step 15). Trader visibility to every player and the message-78 lifecycle remain. |
| (TODO-FEATURES #1) Ship Design tab | a single tap on a design shows its details straight away, including cargo capacity and the full summary figures (`Nova.Client.DesignDetails`) |

**Still open** from the overturns table / "What's next": #2's step-29 waypoint resynchronisation (message 248; the port has no waypoint aimed at a special object - the map picker lists only fleets and planets - so the spec's step 5 is not ported),
#10 production templates, #11 catalog 10l,
#18, #20-#24 combat/damage (and the #19 fuel 39/139 downgrade), #27 Trader visibility / message 78, #31's
server-side cap/name enforcement (blocked on the AI design planner's slot management and name lengths), #35's nine-step planet pass and the rest
of #37's P2/P3/P4 corrections, and the AI rows #36/#38/#40-#41, #42 planet route, and the rest of #44-#51 client work.

## Where spec-11 overturns earlier work

Code (including the three spec-10 implementation passes) that spec-11 now contradicts, plus stand-ins it replaces. Ordered by importance
(gameplay first, then AI, then UI). Size: S = hours, M = a day or two, L = several days.

| # | Item | Spec-11 rule | What the code does now | Files to change | Size |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Minefield and wormhole detection** (two retractions) | a field is seen when known and within r, within p, within r/4, or the fleet is inside it (planets: within r and one of those); two per-race masks (known / seen this year), a sweep marks known only; a wormhole end is seen within r when located, or within r/4, or within p; **no roll**; the located bit is cleared when the end jumps | `ScannerRules.DetectsMinefield` = (range + size + 4)²; `DetectsWormhole` rolls 0-99 against a 75% cloak; "discovered" = a report exists | `Common/ScannerRules.cs`, `ServerState/TurnSteps/ScanStep.cs`, `Minefield` known mask, `Wormhole` located mask, `MineSweepStep`, `ScanningSpec10Test` | M |
| 2 | **Wormhole lifecycle** | base 0-2 + age; tier = base + age/5 - 2 clamped 0-6; a roll below the tier jumps the end anywhere (age 0, located cleared), else age + 1 and a drift of up to 12 ly per axis via 100 scored candidates; pairs by galaxy size (Tiny 0-2 .. Huge 4-8), none under No Random Events; never disappears | `WormholeDriftStep` placeholder chance and nudge, fixed `StabilityTier`, stars / 20 pairs, 30-ly check | `Common/GameObjects/Wormhole.cs`, `WormholeDriftStep.cs`, `StarMapInitialiser.GenerateWormholes`, `WormholeTest` | M |
| 3 | **Minefield routine corrections** | damage exactly equal to the armor does not destroy; detonation exempts only the field owner's own mine-layer hulls, reveals nothing, leaves no wreckage, exact message mapping; the ram-scoop fuel gain is skipped on a hit; 197-200 / 201-204 recipients swapped | `CheckForMinefields.DamageFleet` destroys on `>=`, exempts every owner's layer hulls, `RevealAndLearn` on detonation, wreckage left; `Fleet.Move` credits the scoop first | `ServerState/CheckForMinefields.cs`, `Common/GameObjects/Fleet.cs`, `CombatMinesSpec10RemaindersTest`, `MinefieldCollisionTest` | S |
| 4 | **Mine-sweep rate for gatlings** | R = 4 fixed for Mini Gun / Gatling Gun / Gatling Neutrino Cannon / Big Mutha Cannon (then + 1 on a starbase) | `MineSweepStep.SweepRate` uses `weapon.Range` (Gatling Gun 124 instead of 496) | `ServerState/TurnSteps/MineSweepStep.cs` + the per-weapon table test | S |
| 5 | **Radiating Hydro-Ram Scoop** | only a fleet that moved under its own engines this pass, at the end of its move, passes 0-1; C from the band ends; loss at least 1 unit; message 116 | `RamScoopRadiationStep` hits every fleet carrying colonists whether or not it moved, no minimum | `ServerState/TurnSteps/RamScoopRadiationStep.cs`, a moved-by-engine record in `TurnGenerator.UpdateFleet`, `TurnStepRemaindersH3Test` | S |
| 6 | **Ordinary planets start with 0 surface minerals** | the per-planet loop zeroes the stocks; only planet 0 and the PP/IT second planet start with minerals | `GenerateStars` gives every planet c x rand(0..9) + 10; `NewGameTest` asserts tonnage > 0 | `ServerState/NewGame/StarMapInitialiser.cs`, `NewGameTest` | S |
| 7 | **Galaxy generation** | star count n + (n/4) x (density - 1) (Sparse subtracts) with truncation; fixed 12-ly separation on x-sorted candidates with random trimming; clumping = n random picks with the 4:1 / 2:1 / 1:1 / 1:2 bands and no outer limit; home worlds chosen among the placed stars by the Starting Distance bands | `PresetStarCount` adds only above Sparse with rounding; `PlaceStarsByCount` uses the `StarSeparation` setting; `RelaxStars` walks every star once with inverted weights; `PlaceHomeworlds` density approach, homes first | `Common/Files/GameSettings.cs`, `ServerState/NewGame/StarMapGenerator.cs`, `NewGameOptionsSpec10Test`, `NewGameSetupSpec10Test` | M |
| 8 | **Second planet state** | own concentrations, 100 + rand(0-199) kT each, 10 mines / 4 factories / 0 defences, environment re-rolled to >= 10%, scanner type 0, starbase slot 1, no leftover bonus, not a home world, candidate band 15-23% of D | `InitializeHomeStar` calls `AllocateHomeStarResources` (template, optimum environment, 10/10/10, Scoper 150, adjuster) and `FindNearestUnownedStar` | `StarMapInitialiser.cs`, `NewGameTest` | M |
| 9 | **Simplified setup** | player count by a difficulty x size table with sequential draws; OK writes density Normal, distance by difficulty, flags cleared (Alliances on Expert); no reset-to-defaults command exists; the quick-dialog AI slots follow fixed archetype groups with a single-cycle reorder | `SimplifiedPlayerCountRanges` uniform and difficulty-blind; `ResetToDefaults` Tiny/Sparse/Farther; every computer slot a random archetype | `Nova.Client/NewGameSetup.cs`, `Nova.Avalonia/ViewModels/NewGameViewModel.cs`, `NewGameViewModelTests` | S |
| 10 | **Production templates** | slot 0 is the default (never renamed or cleared); a new empire's slots are empty; applying replaces only the auto-build entries and sets the leftover flag; names <= 12 chars | `ProductionTemplateSet.DefaultSlot`/`NoDefault`; `ApplyTemplate(replace)` deletes everything; "Append" extra; flag untouched | `Common/Production/ProductionTemplate.cs`, `ProductionViewModel.Templates.cs`, `TemplateOrders`, `ProductionTemplateTest` | S |

**Stand-ins confirmed by spec-11 (no change):** the Simplified year gate (2 x size), the JOAT scanner 20E/10E and the NAS order, the 382 trigger,
the 10% salvage decay rounding, the step-21 packet-before-wormhole order, Nova's own turn-step keys, the AR second-planet "not a home world" flag, the
`IsHuman` test, the radiation-raise reading, the exact-rounding terraform edge guard, the packet speed rule (no cap at 13), the Alchemy and 298/303
behaviour, message 63 every turn, the BET exemptions, the WM starting tech, bombing without a tech gain, the mode 1 packet step order, frozen design
prices (Nova was already frozen, the question's premise was wrong), the alliances option-clear path, the AR-visibility stand-in for every archetype
but the Rototills, the salvage-pickup omission, the odd-year P4 alternation and the driverless-starbase skip.

## What spec-11 answers

Of the questions in `behavior-specs-10-questions.md`, spec-11 answers these (the answers are appended to each question there). "Agrees" = the
code already does it; "must change" = the code contradicts the answer.

| Question | Answer in one line | Code |
|---|---|---|
| 0.1 sweep rate | damage x R² per beam slot, R = 4 for gatlings, +1 on a starbase; one-third cut first, then the minimum of 2 | agrees except gatlings - must change |
| 0.2 ram-scoop rate | (86 - C) / 2 % with C from the band ends; only fleets that moved under engines; at least 1 unit | rate agrees; gate and minimum must change |
| 0.3 JOAT scanner | 20E / 10E; NAS doubles the combined normal range only | agrees |
| 0.4 theft | Pick Pocket fleets only, Robber Baron fleets and non-owned planets; colonists stealable; relations ignored | split agrees; colonists must change |
| 1.1 bombing tech gain | never | agrees |
| 1.2 cost estimator | `FUN_10f0_50e0` is the wreckage routine; the AI prices with the doubling | agrees (a stale fragment remains in the spec) |
| 1.3 relation bytes | starting tech levels (WM 6/1/1) | agrees |
| 1.4 Nucleotid | the table was misread: mine output 10, cost 15, mines 5 | must change (data) |
| 1.5 orderable warp | no engine-based limit | agrees |
| 1.6 packet items | up to four | agrees |
| 1.7 AI §7 | retracted entirely | must change (drop the thresholds) |

| 3.2 year gate | raw = 2 x size | agrees |

**Third implementation pass (open items).** Added: mineral packets end to end (production items, launch and merge, flight, decay, arrival, scanning, client reports, destination order, catalog, inspector and map markers); the AI's remaining behaviours (per-personality planet passes, the category 5 starbase branch, personality 4 router/chooser, the packet advisors, role-design queueing, AI starting designs, wormhole intel); the New Game and Race Designer UI (galaxy presets, Simplified/Detailed setup, the AI archetype and tier picker that now actually reaches the AI process, race presets, Random, steppers, locks, working copy, read-only view); the map (nine zoom levels, overlays, picker, Find, tooltips, minefield/wormhole/packet drawing); panels (Detonate order, production templates, Min/Max Terraform, research next field, message routing, Victory Conditions, Technology Browser, report export, Battle Plans fixes, View menu); scrap/cargo caps and wormhole used/tier data. **Pre-existing bugs found and fixed:** battle plans never reached the server; the battle-plan name box renamed on every keystroke; `TemporaryFleets` resurrected dead fleets; a fleet leaving a wormhole drifted with the mouth; scrapped orbiting fleets became wreckage on the planet; the AI Transport design was added every turn; the AI never received its category from the New Game screen; a single mass driver counted as warp+1. **Still open or blocked on the spec:** Route and max orderable warp, the 12-slot ability tally, client-interface row 52 (info-panel modes), AI row 22 and several AI planet-pass rules for personalities 0 and 1, the AI packet distance table, Packet Physics terraform-on-arrival, packet cloaking, the packet speed cap, the desktop Toolbar/Window Layout/Player Colors menus, Change Password, Print Map. **UI caveat:** every UI item in this pass was compiled (Desktop and Android heads, 0 errors) but never run or seen. **New values not from the specs** are tagged "Stand-in" in the rows; the questions are collected in `behavior-specs-10-questions.md`.

**Second implementation pass (same spec-10, after the first pass above).** Added: fleet pursuit, Patrol and Repeat Orders (engine and inspector UI); minefield detonation, victim visibility, Space Demolition design learning, field decay +25, mine sweeping; fleet terraforming; the exact damage word, 30,000 kT wreckage cap, battle movement order; the repair step moved after battle; scanning (SD minefield detection, minefield and wormhole intel, JOAT scanner); Transport instructions, theft, Transfer Fleet, fleet-id reuse; the new-game presets (galaxy size/density/star count/clumping, Maximum Minerals, starting tech upgrades, Starter Colony slots, Potato Bugs, relations); terraform headroom and Min/Max Terraform, the production message set, disaster cleanup, Slow Tech Advance, next-field research, Super Stealth bonus, population carry and decline floor, score history; the AI's starbase advisors, fleet roles, hunter, attack handler, splitter, colony rules, wormhole diversion, the 24 race templates and the design builder; the message filter, research status line, menu bar and accelerators, waypoint selection after delete; economy fixes (mining floor/AR mining and a mine-yield truncation bug, Cost aliasing, Genesis price, at-cap orders, estimator alchemy, bomb cost adjustment) and an advantage-point sampler axis bug. **Values NOT from the specs (please accept or reject):** the mine-sweep rate (community rule), the Radiating Hydro-Ram death rate (Stars! wiki), the JOAT scanner per-level figures 20/10 (community), the Pick Pocket / Robber Baron split (in-game help), the battle movement-order linear curve, the Transport action codes beyond code 7, the minefield detection radius reading. **Not done or blocked:** mineral packets (no packet objects: fleet rows 19, production 26, race-traits 14, AI rows 20/50), Route (spec never says what it does), max orderable warp (spec contradicts itself), the 12-slot ability tally, Maximum-style UI for all the new engine settings (new-game presets, research next field, Detonate order, Terraform Environment catalog), the 20% status-bit cut (copy-protection penalty), design-slot and name caps (your earlier Skip notes), AI personality 4 router/packets, AI category 5 starbase branch, AI skill tiers, desktop View menu. **Verification caveat:** most new tests exercise new APIs, so only a minority were proven to fail without their change; the agents say which they checked (battle/bombing/minefield/wreckage/damage-word mutation proofs done; most others not).

**Implementation status (spec-10 pass; 962 tests green; every new test written from the spec, checked against its fix where noted by the agents).** Done: #1 (AI colonisation bug), #2 (minefields), #3 (home-world concentrations), #4 (Alchemy position), #5 (AR penalty), #6 (PP Energy 4), #7 (Stargate cargo), #8 (colonist unload), #9 (bombardment), #10 (mine laying), #11 (miniaturization/BET), #12 (leftover adjuster), #13 (retracted AI code deleted with its tests), #14 (missile allotment, salvage, battle movement, initiative cap, dump cargo - not the 1/500 damage repack), #15 (Speed Trap 20), #16 (CE +1 Propulsion), the star-name off-by-one; plus the Mystery Trader (creation, movement, encounter, gift templates), the design legality flag with quarter-rate salvage, the integer habitability evaluator, and a pre-existing bug found on the way (slot mass and cost ignored the component count). **Not done:** pursuit/Patrol/Repeat Orders (fleet-targeted waypoints), the home/AR mining floor of 30 and AR innate mining's fixed-10/no-minimum details, the Transfer-dialog drop route (messages 87/88/2), minefield detonation and victim visibility, the Trader's AI planet trade (no skill tiers), the 1/500 damage repack, the AI race templates, starbase-slot advisors, packets, design builder/role tags and personalities 1-5, the Genesis Device cost miniaturization, the research detail-card status line, the message filter, and the desktop menu/accelerators. ## Where spec-9 overturns earlier work

These are places where a prior pass of this project (usually "FIXED" in an earlier report) is now **contradicted by the spec**, or a
pre-existing bug the audit surfaced. They are the natural first implementation targets, roughly ordered by blast radius.

| # | Item | What the code does now | What spec-9 says | Rows |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Beam deflectors do nothing in combat** (pre-existing bug) | `components.xml` property type `"Beam Deflector"` is dropped by `SumProperty`; `BeamDeflectors` reads `"Deflector"` -> always 0 | 0.9 per deflector, truncated on a 1000 scale (90 / 81 / 72) | combat 16 |
| 2 | **AR homeworld shrinks every turn** | `AlternateRealityCapacity` is 100x too low (10,000 on a Space Station) and 2,500 with no starbase | 250,000 ... 3,000,000 colonists; 0 with no starbase; a founded colony gets the "Starter Colony" starbase | pop 12, race 18 |
| 3 | **+10% population bonus on the wrong trait** | gated on Inner Strength (the spec-8 "fix"), test asserts it | **OBRM**; Inner Strength has no population bonus | pop 11, race 31 |
| 4 | **Ultimate Recycling resources** | tested on the fleet owner, 70% (0% without a starbase), credited next turn, persisted | planet owner's trait, 100%, blended into the *same* turn r + d·r/(d+r), remainder lost | fleet 44/45, race 26, prod 30 |
| 5 | **Trait gates skipped on tech-up** (pre-existing bug) | `StarUpdateStep.TechLevelUp` adds crossed components without checking restrictions | gates run first; never replaced by the tech check | research 32 |
| 6 | **`<NRSE>` tags silently dropped** (pre-existing bug) | trait key is `"NRS"`; all seven restrictions ignored | NRSE gates ram scoops / Interspace-10 | research 33, race 29 |
| 7 | **Overgating destroys ships almost always** | A=68 mass curve + two rolls, Interstellar Traveler x0.5 | one roll floor(damage/3)%, IT never vanishes | fleet 37, race 17 |
| 8 | **Scores are wrong** (pre-existing) | counts every token as an Escort, tech scores 0 (`AllTechLevels` never populated), leftover not output resources | exact nine-row formula | victory 16, client-ui 28 |
| 9 | **Colonization / freighter AI** | scored selector with "too eager" roll; 180-ly freighter router | nearest unowned untargeted planet; the 180 ly rule belongs to the warship hunter | ai 4-7, 15-17 |
| 10 | **Regenerating Shields armor** | halves all armor incl. hull; regenerates zero-shield tokens; test locks it in | Armor-category parts only; zero-shield tokens never regenerate | combat 35-39, race 35, ship 39 |
| 11 | **Over-capacity dead band** | 10 colonists | 1,000 colonists (10 units) | pop 16 |
| 12 | **Mining above concentration 100** | raw value in depletion | depletion clamps >= 101 to 100 | pop 23 |
| 13 | **AR planets mine nothing** | 0 operable mines | innate mining, sqrt(pop/100) effective mines | race 19, turn 54 |
| 14 | Battle can grant Mini Morph / Genesis Device | uniform draw over all 12 | only the (unbuilt) Mystery Trader reaches bits 8/10/12 | ship 8, turn 29 |
| 15 | Fuel/repair/bombardment/invasion step order | repair inside movement loop; bombing after colonise; invasion on arrival | steps 22/25, 23b before 23f | turn 1, 21 |
| 16 | Retracted-spec tests | `AiTargetSelectionTest`, `PopulationCapacityTest`, `AlternateRealityCapacityTest`, `UltimateRecyclingDeferredResourcesTest`, `RegeneratingShieldsTest`, `ColonizationTieBreakTest` assert superseded behaviour | - | - |

## Top standout findings (cross-cutting, ranked by how concrete/actionable they are)

1. ~~**Stargates and Mass Drivers have a systematic 2x cost error.**~~ **FIXED.** Confirmed
   exhaustively across all 16 Stargate/Mass-Driver entries *and* all 5 Starbase Chassis entries
   (a second category beyond what was originally sampled) — all had costs exactly half the raw
   binary-extracted values, apparently built from an already-halved "community figure" source
   rather than the real data. All 21 entries' costs have been doubled in `components.xml`, and
   Mass Driver 7 (misnamed "Super Drvier 7" with a wrong tech level) was corrected too.
   *(ship-design-and-components.md)*
2. ~~**Population capacity for growth/crowding isn't scaled by habitability.**~~ **FIXED** —
   `Star.Capacity` now multiplies `race.MaxPopulation` by `habValue` before dividing, so a
   less-habitable world correctly supports fewer colonists. While in the same area: Inner
   Strength's undocumented +10% population-capacity bonus was also found to be wired to the wrong
   trait (OBRM) and has been moved to Inner Strength — see finding below and row 11.
   *(population-growth.md)*
3. ~~**The "population plateaus at capacity" behavior is now directly contradicted by the spec**~~
   **FIXED.** `CalculateGrowth` now implements the spec's traced decline formula past capacity+10
   (ramping smoothly down to a hard-clamped -12%/turn at ~4x capacity); the two tests that asserted
   the old "plateau" behavior were updated to the new decline values, verified by hand-deriving
   the expected numbers from the formula and confirming the tests pass. *(population-growth.md)*
4. **[SPEC-9: REVERSED - see "Where spec-9 overturns earlier work" #4; the same-turn blend is the real rule]** ~~**Ultimate Recycling's scrap-fleet resource bonus is credited immediately, not deferred to next
   turn's production as documented**~~ **FIXED.** The bug was actually worse than "immediate": since
   `ScrapFleetStep` runs before `StarUpdateStep` every turn, and `Star.UpdateResources()`
   unconditionally overwrites `ResourcesOnHand.Energy` from `GetResourceRate()` every time it runs
   (twice per star per turn), crediting the resources share directly into `ResourcesOnHand.Energy`
   got it silently destroyed before it was ever spent, not merely made available a turn early. Added
   `Star.DeferredScrapResources`, credited into `ResourcesOnHand.Energy` only at the very end of
   `StarUpdateStep`'s per-star pass — after this turn's own production spend — so it correctly lands
   in the balance carried into next turn's budget instead. Minerals are unaffected (already credited
   immediately via `+=`, as documented). `Tests/UnitTests/UltimateRecyclingDeferredResourcesTest.cs`.
   *(race-traits.md / fleet-movement-scanning-cargo.md)*
5. ~~**Defense and Terraform base costs are stale.**~~ **FIXED, then found wrong by spec-8, then
   fixed again.** v7's "Defense 44/25/48, Terraform 110/70/120" were **Mineral Packet kilotonnages** read
   off the wrong case group of the cost calculator. Spec-8 settles the real items: Defenses cost the SDI
   record (**15 resources + 5/5/5 kT**, Inner Strength ×3/5 by integer arithmetic), Terraforming **100
   (70 with Total Terraforming, halved for Claim Adjuster)**. The code is back to (effectively) the original
   community figures, now with the minerals Defenses really need. *(production-queue.md rows 8/10)*
6. ~~**Non-ramscoop engines generate zero fuel at any warp**~~ **FIXED.** Every engine now generates
   ≥1mg at warp 1, matching the spec — a stranded fleet with an ordinary engine can self-rescue
   again. *(fleet-movement-scanning-cargo.md)*
7. ~~**Victory check runs before the turn/year counter increment, not after**~~ **FIXED** (evaluation
   now runs after `TurnYear++`) — ~~and the TotalScore victory-condition slider's Maximum (10000) is
   below its own shipped default (11000)~~ **FIXED** (Maximum raised to 20000). Victory conditions
   now have their first test coverage (`VictoryCheckTest.cs`), though most of the file's other rows
   remain untested. *(victory-conditions.md)*
8. ~~**Order-file parsing discards *all* commands for an empire on one bad record**~~ **FIXED** — a
   malformed order now only costs that one order, per the spec's per-record fault isolation.
   *(save-turn-file-format.md)*
9. **[SPEC-9: MOOT - the 180-ly rule belongs to the warship hunter, not the freighter router]** ~~**AI freighter routing's reachability cutoff is off by a factor of 180**~~ **FIXED** — now
   compares against 180²=32,400, matching the spec's intended ~180 ly range. *(ai-opponent-behavior.md)*
10. ~~**A combat targeting formula (`GetAttractiveness`'s beam range-falloff term) is now positively
    confirmed by the spec to be *wrong***~~ **FIXED.** The term has been removed — a beam weapon's
    attractiveness score now depends only on cost/armor/shields/deflectors, matching the spec's
    §4 conclusion (reached via four independent static-analysis techniques) that no range term
    exists in the real targeting formula. *(combat-resolution.md)*
11. ~~**Cloaking is a clean, fully-specified gap**~~ **FIXED.** Replaced the old flat hardcoded 20%
    with `CloakCalculator`'s decompiled piecewise raw-units-to-percent curve, Super Stealth's flat
    +300 raw-unit baseline (300 raw lands exactly on the curve's 75% breakpoint, matching the
    community-known "SS races carry an inherent 75% cloak"), a back-solved +40 baseline for Improved
    Starbases (disclosed as not decompiled the way SS's figure is), `Fleet.RecalculateCloak`'s
    mass-weighted fleet-level combination, and the 18-entry Tachyon Detector counter-cloak table
    applied in `ScanStep`. *(combat-resolution.md)*
12. **Research has almost no dedicated test coverage** (now 5/31, up from 2 — still mostly gaps)
    despite 19/31 behaviors being implemented — tech trading and the Generalized Research split
    still have real code with zero direct tests, though the cost curve now does. ~~The "turns to
    next level" forecast doesn't account for Generalized Research halving the rate~~ **FIXED** —
    see row 29, `Research.TargetFieldContributionFraction`. ~~Bleeding Edge Technology and
    Miniaturization remain literal empty `// TODO` stubs~~ **FIXED** — see rows 11/12,
    `ShipDesign.ApplyMiniaturizationAndBleedingEdge`. *(research-tech-tree.md)*
13. ~~**`race-traits.md` (the largest file) shows most PRT/LRT *secondary* effects still
    unimplemented**~~ **Mostly fixed.** War Monger's combat bonuses, Claim Adjuster's free
    terraforming, and Regenerating Shields are now all implemented (see rows 6/7/35 and finding
    11's cloak fix for Super Stealth/Improved Starbases). Still open: Super Stealth's separate
    minefield-safety/passive-research-sharing pair (its own cloak effect is fixed).
14. ~~**The auto-build population-throttle … still not addressed by any spec revision.**~~ **RESOLVED
    by spec-8, and it exposed a real bug.** The clamp is `min(N, operable(next year's projected population)
    − built)` (production-queue §10a) — the throttle built earlier was essentially right. But the spec's
    live test (§10h) shows **"up to N" is a per-turn maximum, not a standing total**: "Up to 12" with 10
    already built keeps buying every year. `ProductionOrder.Process` treated N as a target and went idle
    once N were built; that is fixed and tested. Auto Mineral Alchemy is also now a persistent "buy as many
    as possible (1,000)" order. *(production-queue.md rows 5/12/22)*
15. **Two new mechanics from spec-7 — resolved differently by spec-8.** (a) ~~"Planet Rebirth"
    disaster~~ **IMPLEMENTED — it is not a disaster at all**: it is the build-completion effect of the
    **Genesis Device**, an ordinary 5,000-resource manual production item; `GenesisDeviceProductionUnit`
    + `Star.ApplyGenesisDevice` + a broadcast message, fully tested (production-queue row 18). (b) **[SPEC-9: RETRACTED - AR has no automatic Mineral Alchemy]** ~~AR
    automatic Mineral Alchemy: still not implementable.~~ The spec did *not* resolve it — turn-generation
    §6 now records a whole-routine search that found no such function and suggests it may be a client-side
    display estimate. See the ambiguity list (item 1). *(production-queue.md rows 13/18)*
16. **The turn order was wrong in the code, because the old spec list was wrong.** Spec-8 rebuilds the
    master routine as 40 steps and states that combat is step 23 — **after** fleet movement and the
    production hub — and that the old "combat at phase 9" list was a coarse misreading. This project had
    moved combat *before* movement on the strength of that list (and earlier audits reported it "confirmed
    fixed, well-tested"). It now runs after movement and production, colonisation resolves after the
    battle, and the year counter / victory check / scan step come last. `TurnOrderTest` (verified to fail
    against the old order). *(turn-generation-engine.md rows 1-3)*
17. **Operable/build limits were mis-specified and Alternate Reality was unrestricted.** The operable
    factory/mine count multiplies before flooring (25,000 colonists operate 25 factories, not 20), is
    floored at 1, and is clamped by a separate **build cap** set by *maximum* population; Defenses have
    their own caps (4 × habitability %, 10..100; one operable per 2,500 colonists); and Alternate Reality
    can build/operate none of them. All implemented (`BuildingCapsTest`). *(production-queue.md rows 3/4/9)*
18. **Alternate Reality population capacity** now follows the spec-8 starbase-chassis table (2,500 /
    5,000 / 10,000 / 20,000 / 30,000) — with one disclosed deviation for planets with no starbase (spec
    says 0; see ambiguity list item 2). AR landings on owned planets now destroy the colonists.
    *(population-growth.md row 12, turn-generation row 31)*
19. **Smaller spec-8 corrections implemented:** minefield decay is now a yearly `2 + 4×S`% pass instead of
    a 1% decay applied once per moving fleet while mutating the collection it iterated; the fuel pass
    (+50 mg per Anti-matter Generator, +200 mg per Fuel Transport ship, *not* ramscoops); the repair-rate
    selection (moved-this-year rule, Inner Strength ×2, fuel-transport bonuses, flat starbase rate);
    Accelerated BBS starting population scaled by growth rate; the 25,000-raw-unit no-cloak guard.

## UI differences for your review

You asked to separate genuine relevance from grey-area cosmetic/architectural differences. Pulling
the two UI-heavy files' rows into one triage table, sorted with clearest-relevance-first:

| Behavior | Source | My read | Why | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Habitability "bullseye" ring overlay on planets | client-interface.md | **Relevant** | Conveys real game state (habitability) visually — same category as the map dots/rings you already prioritized once |  |

| 3-segment mineral concentration/stockpile bar overlay | client-interface.md | **Relevant** | Same — real information not surfaced on the map at all |  |

**Summary**: 45/58 implemented, 45/58 tested. (Row 39 is new in spec-8, row 40 is N/A; AI rows are otherwise unchanged by the spec refresh apart from the §8 preamble noting the AI runs once per game load and its orders reach turn generation as ordinary files.)

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Battle triggers only when co-located races have hostile orders | ✅ | ✅ | `ServerState/BattleEngine.cs:338-357,605-633`. **Spec-11:** covered by `CombatCoverageTest` (rows 1, 4, 5/24, 6, 14, 18). |  |
| 2 | "Legitimate enemies" single Attack category, shared by combat+bombing | ✅ | ✅ | `BattleEngine.cs:828-860`, reused by `Bombing.cs:66`. `Tests/UnitTests/BattlePlanAttackPolicyTest.cs` (5 tests). |  |

| 4 | 256-token cap, fair per-race allocation | ✅ | ✅ | `BattleEngine.cs:418-495`. **Spec-11:** covered by `CombatCoverageTest` (rows 1, 4, 5/24, 6, 14, 18). |  |
| 5 | 16-round cap | ✅ | ✅ | `BattleEngine.cs:41,573-593`. **Spec-11:** covered by `CombatCoverageTest` (rows 1, 4, 5/24, 6, 14, 18). |  |
| 6 | Round-tiered movement (0-3 squares, bonus schedule) | ✅ | ✅ | `BattleEngine.cs:49-78,867-1003`. **Spec-11:** covered by `CombatCoverageTest` (rows 1, 4, 5/24, 6, 14, 18). |  |
| 7 | Weight-based movement order with near-parity randomization | ❌ | ⚠️ | `BattleEngine.cs:890` — explicit TODO, list-order iteration only. **Implemented (spec-10 pass 2):** `BattleEngine.MovementOrder`/`TokenWeight`: heaviest (design mass + share of fleet cargo, x ships) first, then one pass of adjacent pairs where the lighter goes first with chance 0.5 x (1 - difference/0.20). **The linear curve is an interpretation** of "proportionally increasing chance". `BattleMovementOrderTest`. **Spec-11:** **superseded** ("the real band is about 25% and the curve is not linear"). W = the mass of **one ship** of the design plus its per-ship cargo share (not the stack total; starbases have no weight and never move); r = 0-14 per token, drawn at setup and **re-drawn after each round's movement**; E = W + 2 x (r - 7) x W / 100 (truncating, held as unsigned 16-bit); in each of the three steps the engine repeatedly picks the largest E strictly below the previous pick (ceiling 30,000) and moves the tokens with that E that still have enough moves; equal E -> token-array order; E > 30,000 never moves. Chance the lighter moves first: B/A 1.00 -> 50%, 0.95 -> 34.7%, 0.90 -> 21.1%, 0.85 -> 10.7%, 0.80 -> 3.3%, 0.76 -> 0.4%, below 0.755 -> 0 (7/15 - 1/15 - 7/15 at equal weight). `BattleEngine.MovementOrder` sorts by the stack weight (`TokenWeight` x ships) with one pairwise swap pass at 0.5 x (1 - diff/0.20); `BattleMovementOrderTest` asserts the superseded linear rule. Closes question 6.1. |  |
| 8 | Six movement tactics | ⚠️ | ⚠️ | Three "Maximise" tactics collapsed into one; `BattlePlan.cs:69-76` self-documents "only two tactics switched on". |  |
| 9 | Starbase +1 range bonus + fleeing-ships-misjudge-range quirk | ✅ | ✅ | Not found. **Spec-9:** now fully specified: +1 reach for a starbase firer, but the falloff divisor uses the stored range only (a starbase range-1 beam at distance 2 loses 20%). **Implemented (spec-9 pass):** `BattleEngine.WeaponReach` gives a starbase firer +1 reach; `BeamFalloffPercent` divides by the stored range only. `BattleCombatRulesTest`. |  |
| 10 | 7-way target-type classifier | ⚠️ | ✅ | `BattleEngine.cs:683-715`; Freighters/Fuel Transports are a disclosed heuristic substitute. |  |
| 11 | Attractiveness formula | ✅ | ✅ | `BattleEngine.cs:734-798` — **Fixed**: removed the beam range-falloff term the spec's §4 exhaustively confirms doesn't exist in the real targeting formula (it belongs only to the separate beam range-dissipation formula applied to actual damage, §6). `Tests/UnitTests/BattleAttractivenessRangeTest.cs`. **Spec-9:** standard beam score = cost x deflector% / (armor + shields + 1); sapper = ceil(cost x deflector% / shields), 0 (never picked) with no shields; ties go to the earlier token. **Code**: a shieldless target gives the sapper `double.MaxValue` (most attractive), targets are chosen once per stack per round regardless of reach. **Implemented (spec-9 pass):** `GetAttractiveness` weapon-specific overload: standard beam cost x deflector% / (armor + shields + 1), sapper ceil(cost x deflector% / shields), shieldless sapper target scores 0, ties to the earlier token. Still one target per stack per round (shot-time retargeting not done). **Spec-10:** §4 target cost is exact: (resources + boranium) x ships, x100 if under 100,000 else fixed at 10,000,000; armor = ships x armor - carried damage; beam score = 100 x cost x deflector% / 100 / (A + S + 1), sapper ceil(100 x cost / S), missile cost / APN, every score >= 1. `GetAttractiveness` uses per-ship values: no cap, no x100, "+1" per ship, no minimum of 1. **Implemented (spec-10 pass):** target score scaling exactly per spec (whole-token cost, x100 below 100,000 else 10,000,000, beam/sapper/missile scores, minimum 1). `BattleSpec10Test`. |  |
| 12 | Initiative computation (PRT bonus, 0-8 clamp, mass penalty) | ✅ | ✅ | `Common/Components/ShipDesign.cs:463-478` — hull+computer only, no PRT bonus/clamp/mass term. **Spec-10:** initiative is exact: hull initiative + 1/2/3 per Battle Computer/Super/Nexus capped at 63, slot bracket = token + weapon initiative capped at 63, sweep 63 to 0; no trait or random term. `ShipDesign.Initiative` + `WeaponDetails.TotalInitiative` and the hull values match; only the 63 cap is missing. The old "0-8 initiative" is actually battle movement (new row). **Implemented (spec-10 pass):** `ShipDesign.MaxInitiative = 63` caps hull + computer; `WeaponDetails.TotalInitiative` capped at 63. |  |
| 13 | Firing-order tie-break (range then persistent coin-flip) | ⚠️ | ❌ | `ServerState/WeaponDetails.cs:58-69` — unstable sort, not a persisted coin-flip. **Spec-11:** the 0-14 value is **not** a firing tie-break (it is the movement jitter; "no firing code reads it"); within an initiative bracket the dispatcher takes tokens from the last entry of the token array down to the first (shuffle order). The community "range, then persistent coin flip" is contradicted at code level; `WeaponDetails.CompareTo` sorts by initiative then shorter range. |  |
| 14 | Per-weapon-slot firing, ShipsInToken scaling | ✅ | ✅ | `BattleEngine.cs:1445-1471`. **Spec-11:** covered by `CombatCoverageTest` (rows 1, 4, 5/24, 6, 14, 18). |  |
| 15 | Beam range dissipation | ✅ | ✅ | `BattleEngine.cs:1458-1463`. **Spec-9:** exact order of operations: slots x ships x damage, x capacitor%, x deflector%, x (100 - floor(10 d / range)) / 100, every step truncating, Chebyshev distance. `CalculateWeaponPower` uses a continuous 0.1 x min(1, d/range) with Euclidean distance. **Implemented (spec-9 pass):** exact order (slots x ships x damage, x capacitor%, x deflector%, x (100 - floor(10d/range))/100, truncating each step) with Chebyshev distance (`GridDistance`). |  |
| 16 | Beam deflector stacking (0.9^n) | ✅ | ✅ | `Common/Components/ProbabilityProperty.cs:118-137`. **Spec-9:** **BUG (pre-dates spec-9, found by this audit): beam deflectors never take effect.** `components.xml` gives them a `"Beam Deflector"` property that `ShipDesign.SumProperty` has no case for; `ShipDesign.BeamDeflectors` reads the key `"Deflector"` and so always returns 0. Even once wired, `ProbabilityProperty` gives 72.9% at three deflectors where the spec truncates to 72 (90 / 81 / 72 on a 1000 scale). **Implemented (spec-9 pass):** **deflector bug fixed**: `ShipDesign.SumProperty` handles `"Beam Deflector"`, `BeamDeflectorPercent` is 90/81/72/65 on a 1000 scale, `BeamDeflectors` reads it. Includes a test over the real `components.xml` record. |  |
| 17 | Shields absorb before armor | ✅ | ✅ | `BattleEngine.cs:1273-1290,1354-1387`. **Spec-9:** sappers never touch armor and do nothing to a shieldless token; `FireBeam` (line 1315) sends sapper overflow into armor. **Implemented (spec-9 pass):** sappers never touch armor and do nothing to a shieldless token; remaining shield pool re-split per ship (floored). |  |
| 18 | Torpedo/missile independent per-missile hit/miss | ✅ | ✅ | `BattleEngine.cs:1307-1345` — matches spec's worked example exactly. **Spec-11:** covered by `CombatCoverageTest` (rows 1, 4, 5/24, 6, 14, 18). |  |
| 19 | Capital missile double damage post-shield-depletion | ✅ | ✅ | Approximated via all-or-nothing check, not precise prorating (disclosed). **Spec-9:** capital-missile doubling is decided **once per salvo, before any missile resolves**, only if the target pool is already 0; `FireMissile` doubles mid-salvo. Worked Example 3 was redone. **Implemented (spec-9 pass):** doubling decided once per salvo before any missile resolves, only when the pool is already 0; salvos over 200 use the expected-value shortcut; two aggregated damage applications (miss chip, then hits). **Spec-10:** allotment is now exact (n-search with ceil hits: hits for n missiles = ceil(H x n / M); misses x D / 8; hits x D / 2 truncated twice). **Two off-by-ones in `FireMissile`**: miss chip is `misses x (D/8)`, hits use `hit - hit/2` so an odd product keeps the point. **Implemented (spec-10 pass):** `FireMissile`: n-search allotment (`AllotMissiles`, `RollMissileHits`), misses x D / 8, hits x D / 2 truncated twice, leftover missiles re-roll at the next target; new `BattleEngine(ServerData, BattleReport, Random)`. |  |
| 20 | Accuracy formula (jam/computer subtractive cancellation) | ✅ | ✅ | `BattleEngine.cs:1489-1501` uses a simpler approximation, self-flagged as unconfirmed — spec now fully confirms the real formula. **Spec-9:** the formula is now exact: residual = jam - computer; if >= 0, base x (100 - residual)/100, else 100 - (100 - leftover) x (100 - base)/100, clamped 1-100 (base 20 + Battle Super Computer 30 = 44). The hit test `accuracy >= random.Next(0,100)` is likely off by one. **Implemented (spec-9 pass):** `CalculateWeaponAccuracy` is the exact two-branch integer formula, clamped 1..100 (44 / 16 / 28 on the published cases). Hit test changed to `roll < accuracy` (all three spec statements imply it). |  |
| 21 | Whole-ship-kill division + 1/500 armor-quantization exploit | ✅ | ✅ | Correct division, but per-token not per-1/500-quantized (disclosed). **Spec-9:** kill accounting is exact: already-damaged ships die first needing only their remaining armor, the remainder is pooled over the survivors and rounded up to the next 1/500 of armor, every survivor then counts as damaged. **Implemented (spec-9 pass):** one-kill limit via `DamageArmor(killLimit)` with surplus discarded. **Not done**: damaged-ships-first ordering and 1/500 quantisation (single armor total per token). **Spec-10:** the low 7 bits of the damage word are the percentage of surviving ships that carry damage; an exact unpack/kill/repack procedure with a worked example is given. Code still pools armor (`DamageArmor`). **Implemented (spec-10 pass 2):** new `Common/Combat/DamageWord.cs` (7-bit damaged-ship percentage, 9-bit damage in 1/500 of armor, cap 499): unpack, kill damaged ships first, kill limit, repack (percentage rounded up with no leftover; pooled and rounded up to the next 1/500 at 100% with leftover). The spec's worked example reproduces (10 ships, 450 hit -> 9 ships at 39 units). `BattleEngine.DamageArmor` and minefield damage use it; `ShipToken.PackedDamage` persists it (old saves rebuild it). `RepairStep` repairs on the word. `DamageWordTest`, `BattleDamageWordTest`, `PersistedDamageWordTest`. **Spec-11:** the battle repack is unchanged; the outside-battle writers (merge, exchange/split, newly built ships, persistence) are rows 43-45. |  |

At three battle-report sites a race without a salvage success this generation that passes a 50% roll first tries a random rare-part slot (gains the part with that slot's percentage, messages 314/315/318), otherwise a random research field: if the race's level is below the threshold **the price of the next level is added to the research pool** (message 239) - no level is granted. | 18 | ~~Minefield detection radius formula~~ **retracted by spec-11: the "stored value + 4, squared" was the Packet Physics packet scanner (speed field + 4 = warp; warp² = range)** | ❌ | ❌ | Not found. **Implemented (spec-10 pass 2):** `ScannerRules.MinefieldDetectionRadius` = radius + 4; a scanner detects a field when distance squared <= (scan range + radius + 4) squared. **Assumption:** the spec says "stored warning/size value plus 4, squared, as a flat detection radius"; adding it to the scanner's range is an interpretation. New `EmpireData.VisibleMinefields` (owned + struck + scanned) and `CanSeeMinefield`; `IntelWriter` now sends each empire only its visible fields. The Avalonia map still uses its own owner/scan-overlap rule. **Spec-11:** **retracted**. The complete rule: a field not yet seen this generation is detected by a **ship** when (a) it is known and the centre is within r, (b) within the penetrating range p, (c) within **r/4**, or (d) the fleet is inside the field (squared distance <= mine count; no scanner needed); by a **planet** only when the centre is within r **and** (a), (b) or (c) holds; by a PP packet within its range. Detection sets both the *known* (+10) and *seen-this-generation* (+14) masks; a mine hit sets both; a sweep sets known only; nothing clears known; no roll and no cloak % (an unknown field behaves as 75% cloaked, a known one is seen at full range). `ScannerRules.DetectsMinefield` = (range + radius + 4)² (`ScanStep.UpdateVisibleMinefields`; `ScanningSpec10Test.MinefieldDetection_UsesTheFieldsSizePlusFour_AddedToTheScanRange`) implements the retracted reading. Closes question 5.8 (the stand-in was wrong). |  |

| 20 | ~~Wormhole probabilistic detection (75% cloak)~~ **retracted by spec-11: no roll; a per-race located mask and an r/4 rule** | ❌ | ❌ | Deliberately simplified to "visible once in range" (disclosed). **Implemented (spec-10 pass 2):** `ScanStep.DetectWormholes`: in range of a scanner and `Next(100) >= 75` while undiscovered, no roll once discovered; new `WormholeIntel` / `EmpireData.WormholeReports`. Wormholes previously never reached clients. **Implemented (spec-10 pass 3):** reports carry `StabilityTier` and `UsedByUs` (set on transit of either end); the AI diversion scorer reads them. **Ambiguity:** whether the spec's per-race "known" bit is set on transit, on first detection, or both. **Spec-11:** **retracted**: a ship or planet scanner with normal range r sees an end within r when the race's *located* bit is set, **or** the end is within r/4 (squared distance <= r²/16), **or** within the penetrating range; a PP packet sees any end within its range; **no random roll**. The located bit (word +8) is set by detection and by a transit on the exit end only, and cleared for every race when that end jumps, so the "75% cloak" returns after each relocation; the traversed mask (+10, both ends, never cleared) drives the map's connecting line and the AI. `ScannerRules.DetectsWormhole` / `UndiscoveredWormholeCloakPercent`, `ScanStep.DetectWormholes` ("a report exists" = discovered) and `ScanningSpec10Test.UndiscoveredWormhole_IsDetectedOnARollOfSeventyFiveOrMore`, `Wormhole_OutOfRange_IsNeverRolledFor_AndAKnownOneNeedsNoRoll` implement the retracted roll. Closes 5.5. |  |

| 29 | Fixed-amount Load/Unload cargo task | ✅ | ✅ | `Common/Waypoints/CargoTask.cs:180-228`. **Spec-9:** the Transport task is now traced in full (four passes a turn, five slots Fe/Bo/Ge/colonists/fuel, caps on free space and source holdings). **CONTRADICTS**: `CargoTask.cs:145-228`/`Cargo.cs:227-241` clamp nothing (loads can overfill, sources can go negative), any task at a foreign star runs `InvadeTask` (even a Load or mineral unload), minerals cannot be dropped at an unowned star, no fuel slot. **Implemented (spec-10 pass 3):** old `CargoTask` Load/Unload now capped: slots in order Fe, Bo, Ge, colonists, each cut to free space (recomputed per slot) then to what the source holds; unloads cut to what is carried; theft-capable fleets skip the colonist slot instead of failing validation. **Spec-11:** add messages 43/44 (planet-source loads) and the planet-source theft test (Robber Baron only) - see row 35. |  |

Fuel excess at a planet stays aboard (spec silent). **Spec-11:** Load Optimal at a planet hands over nothing (the fleet keeps all its fuel) - confirmed; at a fleet up to its free tank space - agrees. The substitution at an unowned planet (a not-moved own remote-mining fleet stands in as the target) is not modelled (row 78). |  |

| 35 | Theft: Pick Pocket/Robber Baron cargo siphon | ⚠️ | ⚠️ | Not found. **Implemented (spec-10 pass 2):** Pick Pocket / Robber Baron scanner theft from foreign fleets; planets only with a Robber Baron; colonists never stolen. **Assumptions:** the Pick Pocket = fleets / Robber Baron = fleets and planets split is from in-game help text (the spec has one flag); excluding colonists is my choice. `CargoTheftTest`. **Spec-11:** **the exact rule contradicts the code**: Pick Pocket = loads from another race's fleets only; Robber Baron = fleets **and planets not owned by the fleet's owner (another race's or unowned)**; **all three minerals and colonists** can be taken from fleets and planets (the planet's population drops), fuel from fleets; **diplomatic relations are not consulted**; the thief gets 281 for a fleet target, the ordinary 43/44 for a planet, nothing to the victim; refusals 287 planet / 288 fleet / 291 deep space. `CargoInstructions.MayTakeFrom` excludes colonists and always allows loads from unowned planets; `CargoTask.IsValid` likewise; `CargoTheftTest` asserts colonists are never stolen. The unowned-planet rule is held until confirmed (it would stop ordinary freighters collecting remote-mined minerals - new question). Closes 0.4 (the split was right, the colonist exclusion wrong). |  |
| 36 | Stargate overgating damage formulas | ✅ | ✅ | `TurnGenerator.cs:801-856`. `StargateJumpTest.cs`. **Spec-9:** code-confirmed: per design (empty mass), each exceeded limit L gives survival factor (5L - x)/(4L) in 1/10,000ths truncated and multiplied in; damage% = (10,000 - product)/100; refused if d > 5R or mass > 5x a gate's limit (msgs 227/228); survivors take damage% of armor (>= 1 point); msgs 231-234. Missing here: per-step truncation, the 1-point minimum, the messages. **Implemented (spec-9 pass):** integer factors and the 1-point survivor minimum are now in; stack-count quirk deliberately not replicated. **Spec-10:** the stack-count quirk is raw-byte confirmed as original; the spec recommends deciding "wiped out" by whether any ship survives - the code's choice. Question closed. **Spec-11:** the counting detail is restated exactly (N = occupied stacks below 100%, H = at 100%, E = emptied; removed with 231 when N = 0 or N - H - E = 0; empty-fleet and 2-at-100%-plus-survivor examples); the survivors' damage figure wraps modulo 512 in the original and the spec recommends capping it at the armor - `TryStargateJump` destroys at >= full armor, which is consistent with "never survive past 100%". |  |

| 38 | **Pursuit of fleet-targeted waypoints** (spec-10 retracts "no dynamic interception") | ⚠️ | ⚠️ | `Common/Waypoints/Waypoint.cs`. **Spec-10:** a waypoint aimed at a fleet stores target kind 2 and the fleet id; step 10 re-locks a lost target, the end-of-movement refresh copies the target's position into waypoints 1+, a target that jumps through a gate or enters a wormhole freezes pursuers (bit 0x20), step 39 turns lost targets into fixed points (msgs 40/41/42). `Waypoint` has only a string `Destination` and a position - none of this exists. **Implemented (spec-10 pass 2):** engine: `Waypoint` target kind/key/freeze flag (saved), `FleetPursuit.ReLockTargets` (step 10), end-of-movement refresh and arrival, jump freeze for stargate/wormhole, step 39 revalidation with messages 40/41/42; client: `InspectorViewModel.AddWaypoint` aims a tapped own fleet or fleet report (never a starbase or the fleet itself) with `Waypoint.AimAtFleet`, rows read "chasing <fleet>". `FleetWaypointBehaviourTest`, `WaypointOrdersTest`. **Not done:** the split/merge re-lock trigger, the ranking score (random among primary-target matches), step 10 re-pointing of waypoint 0. UI not seen on screen. **Spec-11:** the re-lock is exact (closes 5.10): trigger = the target no longer exists, stands anywhere but the stored x/y, or carries the split/merge mark (byte +0x76 bit 0x20, set in step 6 on fleets touched by exchange/split/merge orders) and belongs to a different player; candidates = every fleet exactly on the stored x/y owned by the original target's owner; score = **total mass** (ships x empty mass + the four cargo holds, no fuel); pass 1 = the heaviest *unclaimed* candidate fitting the pursuer's primary target type in strict mode (**None, Any and Starbase fit no fleet**), equal mass replaced on a coin flip; pass 2 = the heaviest fitting ignoring the claim mark, else a running random pick; the chosen fleet gets the claim mark. Step 10 also re-points waypoint 0 to the orbited planet or deep space, clamps coordinates into the galaxy, and re-syncs special-object targets (message 248 for a drifted unlocated wormhole); it runs at steps 10, 29 and after the special-object pass. Step 39: the whole check is skipped when waypoint 0's task is Transport; 40 target gone, 41 unseen in orbit and not frozen, 42 unseen in deep space **or frozen**. `FleetPursuit.ReLockTargets` picks at random among fits (None/Any/Starbase accept every fleet - the opposite), no claim mark, no split/merge trigger, no waypoint-0 re-pointing or clamp; `Revalidate` decides by orbit only and does not skip Transport fleets. |  |
| 39 | Repeat Orders flag | ✅ | ✅ | Not found. **Spec-10:** now specified (fleet byte 5 bit 0x02, record type 10, reached waypoints recycled). **Implemented (spec-10 pass 2):** `Fleet.RepeatOrders` (saved), `RepeatOrdersCommand`, recycling in `UpdateFleet` (a reached waypoint is re-appended with its task and warp; never the fleet's own waypoint, an intercept leg, or the last waypoint left); checkbox in the inspector. **Interpretation:** the spec says only "recycle a reached waypoint". **Spec-11:** the recycle rule is exact (closes 5.11): the reached waypoint is tested in the end-of-movement refresh (position equals waypoint 1's x/y exactly); an **exact copy** of it (task, settings, warp, target kind and id, fleet target included - unless it carries Patrol with a fleet target) is written into the last slot, only when the fleet has three or more waypoints and the last waypoint's x/y differ from the reached one; the host does **not** require the final waypoint to match the start (code agrees); old waypoint 0 is never recycled. `TurnGenerator.UpdateFleet` (:786-790) excludes *every* fleet-targeted waypoint and lacks the same-point test (`FleetWaypointBehaviourTest.RepeatOrders_AReachedInterceptLegIsNeverRecycled` asserts the wider exclusion). |  |
| 40 | 10-entry waypoint-task table; Patrol/Route/Transfer Fleet absent | ⚠️ | N/A | Confirmed still missing. **Implemented (spec-10 pass 2):** Patrol (`PatrolTask`) and Transfer Fleet (`TransferFleetTask`) added: now 5 of 10 tasks; Route still missing. **Spec-11:** Route and Transfer Fleet are now complete rules (rows 42/43); Patrol refined (row 41). |  |

Inspector offers Patrol with speed and range. Closes 3.5. |  |
| 16 | Anti-clumping relaxation pass | ❌ | ❌ | Not found. **Implemented (spec-10 pass 2):** `StarMapGenerator.RelaxStars`: one pass, weights by squared distance, under 144 moves 4:1 toward self, under 626 to the midpoint, under 1601 1:2 toward the neighbour (spec "~" values used as exact strict bounds); home worlds act as neighbours but never move. **Spec-11:** **weights inverted and the walk differs** (closes 3.6 as a reversal): one pass of as many steps as there are stars, each picking a **random** star (with replacement), finding its nearest other star by d² on current positions (ties to the lower index) and moving **only the picked star** by truncating integer division: d² <= 144 none; 145-324 (4 x own + nb) / 5; 325-625 (2 x own + nb) / 3; 626-1,600 (own + nb) / 2; 1,601+ (own + 2 x nb) / 3, no outer limit; stars re-sorted by x afterwards; clumping can bring stars closer than 12 ly. `RelaxStars` walks every star once in order with `< 144` 4:1 self, `< 626` midpoint, `< 1601` 1:2 toward the neighbour, else nothing. The original clumps before home worlds are chosen (they are ordinary stars); Nova's fixed-neighbour home worlds are a port choice (new question). |  |

### production-queue.md

**Spec-8 changed this file more than any other (+159 lines):** a full item-type catalog (§10), the
corrected Defenses/Terraform costs, the operable-count formula and the build cap, the eight queue status
codes, a live-tested meaning for auto-build "up to N", and the resolution of "Planet Rebirth" (it is the
Genesis Device). **Two of spec-7's "fixes" turned out to be built on a misreading and were reverted**
(rows 8 and 10), and the auto-build semantics this project had built were wrong (row 22).

| # | Behavior | Impl | Test | Evidence | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Population resources = floor(pop/setting) | ✅ | ✅ | `Common/GameObjects/Star.cs` `GetResourceRate`. **Spec-11:** covered by `ProductionCoverageTest` (rows 1, 2, 7, 15, 17, 36). |  |
| 2 | Resources per factory + build cost (factory Germanium `4 − flag`) | ✅ | ✅ | `Common/RaceDefinition/Race.cs:330-334`. **Spec-11:** covered by `ProductionCoverageTest` (rows 1, 2, 7, 15, 17, 36). | Tutorial-mode all-minerals `2 − flag` variant not modelled (tutorial excluded). |
| 3 | **Operable factories/mines = `min(buildCap, max(1, floor(setting × pop / 10,000)))`**, pop in units of 100; Alternate Reality 0 | ✅ | ✅ | **Corrected.** `Star.OperableBuildings` / `GetOperable{Factories,Mines}` / `GetFutureOperable*` — product before flooring (25,000 colonists operate 25 factories, not 20), floor of 1 for a populated planet, clamp to the build cap, AR gets 0. `Tests/UnitTests/BuildingCapsTest.cs`. **Spec-9:** confirmed. Note the AR "0 operable mines" also zeroes AR **mining** here, where the spec's live test shows AR mining with effective mines = sqrt(population / 100) (24 mines at 57,700 colonists). |  |
