| 34 | **BET: 2x cost on unmet prereqs, differentiated miniaturization** | ✅ | ✅ | **Fixed**: see research-tech-tree.md row 11/12 - `ShipDesign.ApplyMiniaturizationAndBleedingEdge`. `Tests/UnitTests/MiniaturizationAndBleedingEdgeTechnologyTest.cs`. **Spec-10:** **CONTRADICTS in five places** (`ShipDesign.ApplyMiniaturizationAndBleedingEdge`): hull and starbase-chassis cost is never miniaturized (`Summary = new Component(Blueprint)`); a part with no requirement should use the race's lowest tech level as its margin; rounding is `Resources * double` where the spec uses MulDiv round-to-nearest per field with a floor of 1 per non-zero field; WM/IS/IT/CE adjustments run before miniaturization (spec: after); `ScrapTask.FleetCost` includes BET doubling (the suppress flag means scrap and AI estimates are never doubled). `MiniaturizationAndBleedingEdgeTechnologyTest.NoTechRequirement_...` asserts the superseded behaviour. Terraforming and planetary scanners/defences are excluded; the Genesis Device is included. **Implemented (spec-10 pass):** `ShipDesign.AdjustedItemCost` (miniaturization -> WM/IS/IT/CE -> BET doubling last), hulls and chassis included, no-requirement parts use the race's lowest level, MulDiv round-to-nearest with a floor of 1 per non-zero field, Terraforming/planetary scanners/defences excluded, `CostWithoutBleedingEdgeDoubling` for scrap. `MiniaturizationAndBleedingEdgeTechnologyTest` rewritten. Not done: the Genesis Device cost is not miniaturized; Nova.Ai estimates should use the un-doubled cost. **Implemented (spec-10 pass 2):** the Nova.Ai "estimates should use the un-doubled cost" item is N/A: fleet-movement "Design stored costs" says the AI planners read the STORED (doubled) cost; the "computer player cost estimator" was corrected to be the battle-wreckage routine (now using the un-doubled cost). War Monger / Inner Strength +/-25% now also applies to bombs. The Genesis Device cost is now adjusted. Stale text remains in `ShipDesign.CostWithoutBleedingEdgeDoubling`'s comment and in race-traits.md section 7. **Spec-11:** `FUN_10f0_50e0` is the **battle-wreckage routine** (not an AI cost estimator); the suppress flag is raised by the Transport-task handler for its Scrap Fleet and Colonize recoveries and by the wreckage routine - **no AI routine sets it**, so the AI's production planners price designs **with** the doubling (reverses the spec-10 "AI estimates are never doubled"). `BattleEngine.cs:1470` (`CostWithoutBleedingEdgeDoubling`), `ScrapTask.FleetCost`, `ColoniseTask.cs:141` and Nova.Ai reading `ShipDesign.Cost` agree; the `ShipDesign.cs:1215-1217` comment is stale. The step-7 "uninitialised margin" quirk is resolved: in production the leftover word is always 2, so planetary scanners cost their listed price and Terraform / Defenses never go through the routine - exempt Terraforming parts and planetary scanners/defences from both miniaturization and doubling (confirmed; `IsExcludedFromMiniaturization` does). Closes 1.2 and 7.12. |  |
| 35 | **RS: shields +40% & 10%/round regen, armor 50% effective** | ✅ | ✅ | **Fixed**: `ShipDesign.Update` applies the +40%/-50% design-level multipliers; `BattleEngine.ApplyRegeneratingShields` restores 10% of MAXIMUM shields (not current) once per battle round. Implementing this surfaced and fixed an adjacent real bug: `ShipToken`'s constructor set `Shields` to a single ship's rating instead of the whole token's total, unlike `Armor` (which already scaled by `Quantity`) - every consumer (`BattleEngine`'s damage/attractiveness math, `TurnGenerator`'s per-turn recharge step) already assumed the totalled convention, so a freshly-built or just-split/merged multi-ship token understated its own total shields until the next turn's recharge step ran, parallel to the already-fixed "fresh ship shows 0 armor" bug. `Tests/UnitTests/RegeneratingShieldsTest.cs`. **Spec-9:** code-exact: shields + floor(2/5 x total) capped at 65,535; the armor penalty halves **only Armor-category parts** per slot (not hull base armor, Croby Sharmor, Langston Shell, Multi Cargo Pod); regeneration from round 2, only for tokens with shields above 0. `ShipDesign.cs:895-898` halves everything and `ApplyRegeneratingShields:628` regenerates zero-shield tokens; `RegeneratingShieldsTest.cs:54-61` locks the hull-armor halving in. **Implemented (spec-9 pass):** see combat rows 38/39. |  |

### save-turn-file-format.md

| # | Behavior | Impl | Test | Evidence | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1-4, 6, 7, 9, 12-14, 20-21 | Binary wire-format/opcode/checksum mechanics | N/A | N/A | Deliberate XML format divergence — not applicable. | Agreed, skip |
| 3 | **Design slot caps: 16 hull + 10 starbase** | ❌ | ❌ | Constants exist (`GlobalDefinitions.cs:152-153`) but never enforced anywhere. **Spec-11:** was "Skip"; the spec now recommends enforcing 16 + 10 in the client (the designer's Copy button is enabled only while a free slot exists) and **rejecting** a bad record on the host (a slot above 25, a foreign race, or **replacing a design that is in use and has ships in existence** fails the record; the original aborts the whole generation). `DesignCommand.IsValid` checks only duplicate/missing keys and AR protection; `Global.MaxStarbaseDesignsAmount = 10` is unused. Closes 10.6 (design half). | Skip |
| 5 | **Name fields capped at 31 characters** | ❌ | ❌ | No cap found anywhere. **Spec-11:** 31-character design names now have a stated enforcement point (the designer's edit limit; a 32-byte host field, longer packed names fail the record); fleet and battle-plan names are likewise 31 on the client. Not enforced anywhere (no `MaxLength` in the designer view). | Skip |
| 8 | **Score-history retained for 100 turns** | ✅ | ✅ | Only current-turn snapshot computed, never retained. **Implemented (spec-10 pass 2):** `ScoreHistory` keeps the most recent 100 years, held in `ServerData.ScoreHistory` (saved/loaded), recorded each turn after the victory check. Server side only (row 17's visibility rules not done). **Spec-11:** re-worded: **the host keeps nothing historical** - step 35 recomputes the current record of every race in memory and the host file receives none; the 100-year history is a *client* structure (up to 101 entries per race merged from the records each turn file brings, written to a `.h<N>` history file on save, read back on load); a server may keep the history itself provided it sends a player only what the visibility rules allow. `ServerData.ScoreHistory` is recorded but never written to any client (`Intel.AllScores` carries only the current turn). Closes 10.5 (history half). | Implement |
| 10 | **Incoming record replay: per-record fault isolation, not all-or-nothing** | ✅ | ✅ | **Fixed**: `OrderReader.cs:114-160`'s per-node parsing now has its own inner try/catch, so one malformed order only costs that order — the rest of the file's commands (and the empire's `TurnSubmitted` flag) still land. `Tests/UnitTests/OrderReaderFaultIsolationTest.cs`. **Spec-11:** per-record fault isolation is now the spec's own recommendation ("reject rather than abort on a bad record"). | Fix |
| 11 | Full state-dump per-race snapshot | ⚠️ | ⚠️ | `IntelWriter.cs:61-136` — XML equivalent exists; score-history component absent (see #8). **Spec-11:** the score component is present but unfiltered (row 17). | Agreed, sufficient |
| 15 | **Orders accepted only for the host's current turn / game / player; stale or foreign files silently skipped; failed opens retried up to ~4 s** (spec-8 §10.6, turn-generation §1a) | ✅ | ✅ | `OrderReader.ReadPlayerTurn`: a file whose `Turn` differs from the host's, or whose `Id` is another empire's, is skipped silently; the locked-file retry loop exists. Exercised indirectly by `OrderReaderFaultIsolationTest`. **Spec-11:** covered by `TurnGenerationCoverageTest` (save-format row 15). | The original also rejects a *newer* turn with an error and checks a per-generation nonce; neither applies to XML orders. |
| 16 | **Header record, file-kind byte, version range, per-generation nonce and the keystream cipher** (spec-8 §10: there is no compression — every payload except types 0/8 is XORed with a keystream seeded from public header fields) | N/A | N/A | Deliberate XML format divergence. | Agreed, skip |
| 17 | Score-history record (opcode 0x2d, 24 bytes/race) written per player under the own-race / game-over / eliminated / Public-Scores-after-turn-19 rule | ❌ | ❌ | Not found (same gap as row 8). **Spec-11:** the rule is exact (closes 10.5): a player's turn file carries race k's 24-byte record for the new year only when (1) the game is over (victory bit set), (2) k is the player's own race, (3) k is eliminated ("so its final standing is shown to everyone"), or (4) "Public Player Scores" is on and the new turn counter exceeds 19 (from the file for 2420 on); otherwise another race's score never reaches the player. `IntelWriter.WriteIntel:105` gives every player every race's record every turn; needs a `GameSettings.PublicPlayerScores` flag (with a New Game checkbox) and a persisted game-over/winner flag (victory row 11). |  |
| 18 | Score visibility per player: own race / game over / eliminated / Public Player Scores after turn counter 19; the host keeps no history; the client keeps up to 101 years per race in a history file | ❌ | ❌ | `IntelWriter.WriteIntel` (row 17); no history reaches the client. |  |
| 19 | Full-detail fleet records (own fleets in the turn file, every fleet in the host file) carry a 16-bit mask of damaged stacks and one damage word per marked stack; other players' fleet records carry no damage; the loader cuts a figure above 499 to 499 | ⚠️ | ⚠️ | `FleetIntel.cs:235` copies `PackedDamage` for every scan level (own-only not enforced; combat row 42). |  |
| 20 | Opcode 0x2e = the race's default production template (flag byte, count 0-12, 12 words), appended when the client's slot 0 differs from the race-record copy - not a display name | N/A | N/A | Equivalent via `ProductionTemplateCommand` (whole slots); its "default slot" mode is moot once slot 0 is fixed (production row 23). | Reverses the spec-10 reading. |
| 21 | Opcode 0x1e = battle-plan write or delete (one entry of the acting player's table of up to 16 plans of 36 bytes: settings + 31-char name; index may not exceed the count; a write naming another player is refused; an out-of-range delete is accepted with no effect) | N/A | N/A | XML orders; `Global.MaxBattlePlans = 16` and the 31-char plan name match (ship-design row 46). | Reverses the spec-10 "design component-slot write" reading. |

**Summary**: 6/20 implemented, 5/20 tested (rows 15 and 17 are new in spec-8, row 16 is N/A; spec-8 §10 is almost entirely binary-format detail). Row 10's order-file fault-isolation bug was fixed previously.

**Summary**: 40/47 implemented (full or partial), 34/47 tested (full or partial). (Spec-8 changed this file only in §9 and one cross-reference; row 38's Genesis Device gained its gameplay effect via production-queue.md.)

**What changed since the previous pass**: The single most significant finding is a clean, exhaustively-verified correspondence between two independent gaps: `ServerState/BattleEngine.cs`'s `GrantBattleTechGains` explicitly documents (in its own comment) that it deliberately does *not* reproduce the original's per-component battle-reward table, and — checked component-by-component against the spec's newly-added §14a table — **all 12 of the specific one-time battle/event-granted components it names** (Multi Cargo Pod, Multi Function Pod, Langston Shell, Mega Poly Shell, Alien Miner, Hush-a-Boom, Anti Matter Torpedo, Multi Contained Munition, Mini Morph, Enigma Pulsar, Genesis Device, Jump Gate) **are entirely absent from `components.xml`**, with zero false positives or false negatives against that list — this single root cause explains nearly every "missing component" finding scattered across rows 25–38. The second major finding is a systematic, exactly-2x cost error affecting two full categories in `components.xml`: all 16 Stargates/Mass-Drivers and all 5 Starbase Chassis have resource+mineral costs exactly half of `component-stats.tsv`'s ground truth (e.g. Space Station: xml res=600 vs tsv res=1200). The spec's own §15c explicitly investigated and resolved this exact discrepancy for the Stargate/Driver category, concluding the doubled tsv value is the real in-game figure and that commonly-published/wiki numbers are the ones already halved — meaning `components.xml` was very likely built from those halved wiki figures for these two categories specifically (regular ship hulls, checked as a control, show no such halving — this Starbase Chassis half of the bug is new, beyond what the spec itself flagged). A third, smaller but concrete bug: Inner Strength's documented 5-bomb exclusion (Smart/Neutron/Enriched-Neutron/Peerless/Annihilator) is only enforced for 2 of the 5 (Peerless, Annihilator) in `components.xml` — Smart Bomb, Neutron Bomb and Enriched Neutron Bomb have empty `<Race_Restrictions/>` and are currently buildable by Inner Strength races. A fourth: "Mass Driver 7" is misnamed "Super Drvier 7" (wrong family name, typo, and a wrong tech-level value). A fifth: all 4 Snooper planetary scanners have `PenetratingScan` hard-set to exactly half of `NormalScan`, where the tsv's ground truth has penetrating range equal to the full normal range for all four. Also newly confirmed clean: the previously-RETRACTED "9-component-slot cap" claim is fully resolved — the real per-hull slot count (2–16) is already correctly implemented via `HullModule`, and Meta Morph's specific slot-capacity layout (3/8/2/2/2/2/1) matches the spec's own corrected worked example exactly. No regression tests exist anywhere in `Tests/` for the scanner 4th-power combination formula, `RaceRestriction`/`RaceComponents` availability gating, or any of `ShipDesign.Update()`'s per-component summation logic, despite all three being real, exercised code paths.

---

### turn-generation-engine.md

**This file was substantially rewritten in spec-8.** Its old 23-item "phase list" was replaced by a
40-step reconciled order read statement-by-statement from the master routine, and the spec now says
outright that several old items (combat at phase 9, diplomacy decay, tech-field reveal, hull-upgrade
cascade, autosave, ramscoop refuel, "two identical random-event passes", the squared per-design
"capability rating") were **wrong**. The table below is therefore re-cut around the new steps rather
than patched row-by-row; the old rows it supersedes are noted.

| # | Behavior (spec-8 step) | Impl | Test | Evidence | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Master routine is a strict 40-step ordered pipeline (§1) | ⚠️ | ✅ | `TurnGenerator.Generate()` is still coarser than 40 steps but now follows the reconciled *relative* order: orders → scrap → movement → minefield decay → production steps → **battle** → colonisation resolution → bombing/wormhole/decay steps → year++ → victory → scan/output. `Tests/UnitTests/TurnOrderTest.cs`. **Spec-9:** the 40-step order itself is **unchanged**; these *code* deviations exist (several pre-date spec-9 but were not flagged): fuel (22) and repair (25) run inside the per-fleet movement loop (`ProcessFleet` -> `RegenerateFleet`), i.e. before minefield decay, production and the battle - fleets are healed *before* they fight; orbital bombardment (23b) must precede colonise/invade (23f) but `BombingStep` (key 19) runs after `ColonizationResolver`; invasion happens on arrival during movement, so a starbase destroyed in battle does not open the planet the same turn; wormhole relocation (21) and salvage decay (18) run after the battle, and salvage decay ignores the one-year grace flag. **Spec-11:** the step numbers are "a reading aid, not stored anywhere"; what a port must keep is **14 ordered pairs**: orders first (shuffle only for orders); follow propagation after orders, before movement; Scrap and waypoint-0 colonise/invade before movement; special-object mode 0 (packets, Trader) before movement, ending with a waypoint resync; movement before the home-world flag, salvage/minefield decay, IS growth and the hub; decay (18) before the hub (20), hub before the mode-1 pass (21); hub before fuel (22) and battle (23b); **battle, then bombardment, then the Trader encounter, then remote mining and mine laying, then the second invasion pass (23f), then Transfer Fleet**; laying before sweeping; battle and minefield hits before repair; waypoint resync and population refresh after every mover; counter (33) before victory (35) and output (39); design pass (36) before the visibility passes; file moves, nonce, writing; Patrol re-targeting before each player's file. "A step list with different numbers but this relative order (for example 23 for the Trader encounter, 24 Transfer Fleet, 25 mine sweeping, 26 repair, 28 terraforming, a design-legality pass late in the turn and Patrol re-targeting at file output) matches the original" - Nova's keys verbatim; a separate radiation step must sit after movement and before 17-20 (key 8 does). **Still violated**: pair 8 - `Generate()` runs `ColonizationResolver.ResolvePendingColonizations` (line 349) **before** `RunTurnSteps(step >= BOMBINGSTEP)` (line 352), so a starbase destroyed in battle opens the planet but bombing happens after the landing; and pair 3 (waypoint-0 invasion resolves only post-battle, row 9). The 14 pairs are the new oracle for `TurnOrderTest`. Closes 10.2. |  |
| 2 | **Battle pass runs AFTER movement (16) and the production hub (20), not before (step 23)** | ✅ | ✅ | **Fixed (regression of an earlier fix).** The v5/v7 audits said "combat before movement — confirmed fixed", on the strength of the now-superseded 23-phase list; `Generate()` had `battleEngine.Run()` *before* the movement loop. It now runs after `ProcessFleet` movement and the production steps (keys 11/12) and before bombing (19). A fleet that arrives now fights the same turn; ships built this turn can be fought at their build location. `TurnOrderTest.Battle_RunsAfterTheProductionSteps_AndBeforeTheLaterSteps` (verified to fail with the old position). **Spec-11:** confirmed by pair 7 (hub before battle). | Waypoint *tasks* still execute during movement (see row 9) rather than after battle, so "a fleet destroyed in battle does none of its post-battle tasks" is not yet reproduced. |
| 3 | Year counter (33), victory (35) and output/scan (39) come after all simulation | ✅ | ✅ | `Generate()`: `TurnYear++` then `victoryCheck.Victor()` now run after every production/battle/bombing/decay step; `ScanStep` (key 99) is last. `TurnOrderTest.YearCounterAndVictoryCome_...`. **Spec-11:** confirmed by pair 12 (counter before victory and output). | Previously the year/victory ran *before* the production steps, so victory saw a pre-production galaxy. |
| 4 | Shuffle player order once per turn (step 5), used only by order application | ⚠️ | ✅ | Partially: shuffled order drives fleet processing and `RemoteMiningStep`, not order application. `TurnGeneratorTest.Generate_PopulatesShuffledEmpireOrder_WithEveryEmpire`. | |
| 5 | Step 6: orders applied per record; a bad record only costs itself | ✅ | ✅ | `TurnGenerator.ReadOrders()/ParseCommands()`; per-record fault isolation (standout finding #8). The original aborts the *whole run* on one failing record — this project deliberately does better. **Spec-11:** the two ledgers are laid out (pending-landing record 12 bytes; cross-owner transfer record 25 bytes) - N/A for XML orders; two behaviours are new: a later load order reduces a pending-landing record made by an earlier unload (N/A: Nova applies Transfer-dialog cargo at once) and the transfer messages 66/70/74 to the first object's owner, 68/72/76 to the second (fuel 67-77), row 67. Test: `TurnGenerationCoverageTest` (row 5). | Stale/foreign-game/nonce file checks are binary-format concerns, N/A for the XML order files. |
| 6 | Step 7: duplicate/invalid registration flags and notices 256/257/259 | ❌ | ❌ | Not found. | The spec deliberately does not describe the comparison (identity-block content), so there is nothing to implement. |
| 7 | Step 8: design normalisation (slot 0 forced to an engine) | ✅ | ✅ | Not found. **Spec-11:** **this row was stale**: `DesignNormalisationStep` exists and runs right after the orders are applied (`TurnGenerator.cs:256`): an empty engine slot gets one Quick Jump 5, a non-engine in the engine slot is replaced keeping its quantity, a design with an engine is left alone. `EngineRowsT3Test.DesignNormalisation_*` (3 tests). | Design-editor rules already prevent the state; low value. |
| 8 | Steps 9-11: follow-fleet orders (mark, propagate up to 8 passes, message 312) | ✅ | ✅ | No follow-fleet waypoint exists. **Spec-11:** **this row was stale**: `FollowOrderStep` (steps 9 and 11, `TurnGenerator.cs:263`): a follower copies its leader's onward leg and stays marked; a chain settles over several passes; a chain longer than eight passes leaves the tail unsettled with message 312; a leader going nowhere or gone gives 312 and the mark is lost; the end-of-movement refresh skips a marked fleet's fleet-targeted legs. `EngineRowsT3Test.Follow_*` (5 tests). Spec-11 pair 2 (follow after orders, before movement) is met. | A feature gap in the waypoint model. |
| 9 | Step 12 pre-movement stage: waypoint-zero tasks (Scrap only), colonise/invade resolution, research buy loop, cargo-transfer report | ⚠️ | ⚠️ | `ScrapFleetStep` runs pre-movement (matches mode 1). All *other* tasks run inside `UpdateFleet` during movement; colonisation resolves once (post-movement), not twice; research buys once, not three times. | Closest structural gap to the spec: tasks should be split into pre-move (modes 1/2) and post-battle (modes 3/4). |
| 10 | Step 13: race-definition integrity check with degrade loop (messages 279/386) | ✅ | ✅ | Not found; race legality is enforced only at design time in the race designer. **Spec-11:** **this row was stale**: `RaceIntegrityStep` (`TurnGenerator.cs:285`, after the pre-movement stage, before movement): a legal race is untouched; an out-of-range value is clamped and the race degraded to at least 500 points in the order colonists-per-resource, growth, research classes, stopping as soon as 500 is reached; both notices go out; computer players exempt; a negative total is illegal even with every value in range. `EngineRowsT3Test.RaceIntegrity_*` (6 tests). |  |

| 19 | Step 23: post-movement stage - battle, **orbital bombardment** (`FUN_10f0_6ea2`), Mystery Trader encounter, tasks modes 3/4, colonise/invade again | ✅ | ✅ | Battle ✅ (row 2). The meeting/exchange pass and Trader encounter ❌. **Spec-9:** `FUN_10f0_6ea2` is **orbital bombardment**, not a colonist/mineral "exchange": it kills at least 1 colonist, scales by defence and reverses up to 500 points of terraforming. `Bombing.cs`/`BombingStep` exist (min-kill present); the 500-point un-terraform is missing, the Trader encounter is absent, and the order is wrong (row 1). **Spec-10:** **orbital bombardment is now a complete rule** (§3): gate = the bomber's Attack Who vs its own opinion of the owner, any starbase blocks all bombing; same-owner fleets at a planet are pooled; totals N (normal kill, tenths of a percent), M (minimum kill: 3 per bomb of subtypes 0-4 and per MCM, 20 per Orbital Construction Module), D (installations), S (smart kill, compounding), R (Retro bombs); defence pass-through F = (1 - c/1000)^K and F_s = (1 - c/2000)^K with K = min(defences built, operable); population smart deaths first then normal with stochastic rounding; installations factories f x D / T, defences d x D / T, mines the rest; terraform reversal up to 500. **CONTRADICTS** `Bombing.cs`: one fleet at a time, no pooling, uniform loss across installations, no Retro bombs, coverage from `star.Defenses`, smart coverage = population coverage, OCM/MCM do not count to the minimum, free-text messages, and the owner message is addressed after `Owner = Nobody`. **Implemented (spec-10 pass):** `Bombing.BombAll`: pooled same-owner fleets, N/M/D/S/R totals, defence pass-through with K = min(built, operable), smart then normal deaths with stochastic remainder, per-type installation loss (mines the rest), Retro terraform reversal up to 500, owner message before `Owner = Nobody`; `BombingStep` no longer filters on `HasBombers`. `BombingRuleTest` (17 tests incl. the worked example). Not done: the tech-gain roll after bombing. Data fixes: Lady Finger PopKill 0.2 -> 0.6, Hush-a-Boom MinimumKill 300 -> 0. **Spec-11:** overshoot exact (closes 6.5): the factory and defence extra units are rolled independently and their sum exceeds D **by at most 1**, not 2 (only when m x D / T plus the two fractional parts is at most 1, e.g. a planet with no mines); factories + defences then lose D + 1, mines nothing, and the installation total reported is D. `Bombing.ProportionalLoss` replicates the draws (mechanics agree); the message uses `InstallationsDestroyed` = f + d + m = D + 1 in that rare case (optional one-line change). |  |
| 20 | Step 24: mine sweeping by fleets and starbases | ⚠️ | ⚠️ | Not found (no sweep rate, no sweeper). **Implemented (spec-10 pass 2):** see fleet-movement row 59 (`MineSweepStep`, key 25). **Spec-11:** **the complete sweep rule** (closes 0.1 - the rate is no longer a community value): per installed beam-weapon slot add count x damage x R², R = the beam's battle range, **a fixed 4 for a gatling-type beam** (Mini Gun, Gatling Gun, Gatling Neutrino Cannon, Big Mutha Cannon) whatever its listed range, the slot skipped for a shield sapper, **plus 1 to R on a starbase**; fleet rate = sum of ships x design rate, no tech/trait/plan modifier, no cap; sweepers = every fleet with a positive rate in table order, then every starbase, each against every foreign field it sits inside (fleets by the Attack-Who test, starbases against any non-Friend), the full rate to each field; amount = rate, divided by 3 truncating against a Speed Bump field, **then** at least 2, capped at mines - d² + 1 and at the field; a field at 0 is deleted; otherwise sweeping sets the field's *known* mask only; messages 194 / 244 / 190 on every sweep. Per-weapon ship figures: Laser 10, X-Ray 16, Mini Gun 208, Gatling Gun 496, Disruptor 676, Big Mutha Cannon 3,264; a starbase with eight Lasers sweeps 320. `MineSweepStep.SweepRate` uses `weapon.Range` for gatlings too (`MineSweepStep.cs:169`; Gatling Gun 124 instead of 496); the cut-then-floor order, sappers, the starbase +1 and the caps agree. | Concrete rules in spec §11 (rate ≥2, ⅓ vs third type, messages 194/244/190). |
| 21 | Step 25: repair — moved 1%, deep space 2%, foreign orbit 3%, own planet 5%, own starbase 8% / 20% with dock; Inner Strength ×2; fuel-transport stacks +5/+10%; starbase self-repair 10% (15% IS) | ✅ | ✅ | **New/fixed.** `TurnGenerator.RegenerateFleet(fleet, movedThisYear)`. The spec's 5/10/15/25/40/100 are the same figures in fifths of a percent, so the existing 1/2/3/5/8/20% table was right; what was missing was the *moved-this-year* rule (the old code used "has a waypoint"), IS doubling, fuel-transport bonuses and the flat starbase rate (old: a starbase repaired itself at 8%/20% as if it were a ship orbiting it). `Tests/UnitTests/FleetRepairRateTest.cs`. **Spec-9:** exact rule: units are 1/500 of a ship's armor (the 1/2/3/5/8/20% reading is confirmed); fleets that fought or hit a minefield this generation are **skipped**; at its own planet whose starbase fought a fleet gets 5%; the fuel-transport bonus is **per fleet** (25 if any stack is a Fuel Transport, 50 if any is Super-Fuel, 50 - not 75 - with both), added after the Inner Strength doubling, applying even if the fleet moved; starbase self-repair is skipped if it fought. **CONTRADICTS** in `RegenerateFleet`: runs at step 16 not 25, no skip for fighters/mine-hit fleets, the transport bonus is added per *stack* (+5/+10% each), the "starbase fought" case is a TODO, `Math.Max(..., 1)` adds a 1-armor minimum. **Implemented (spec-9 pass):** the per-fleet fuel-transport bonus (max, not additive) and "no repair after a jump" are in. **Still open**: the skip for fleets that fought or hit a minefield, the starbase-fought 5% case, the 1-armor minimum and the step 16 -> 25 reordering. **Implemented (spec-10 pass 2):** new `RepairStep` (key 26, after the Mystery Trader step 23, Transfer Fleet 24 and mine sweeping 25): repair no longer runs inside movement, so fleets are not healed before they fight. Skipped: fleets in this turn's battle reports, fleets that hit a minefield (including detonation victims) or completed a Stargate jump. 5% at your own planet whose starbase fought (a starbase that fought does not repair itself); the 1-armor minimum is gone; repair works on the damage word. `FleetRepairRateTest` rewritten to the step API. **Spec-11:** pair 10 (battle and minefield hits before repair) confirms the "fought or hit" skip. | "Not counted while the starbase is under attack" TODO remains. |

## Ambiguities and missing information for the next spec pass

**Spec-11 note.** The lists below are the spec-10 audit's and are kept for history. Spec-11 answered items 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7 (Nova was already
frozen), 8 (the spec now argues for the original insertion rule), 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 14 and most of 15 (the resource cut, the packet distance
table, the P0/P1 passes, §7, the D/H roles) of the "Still missing" list, and the Nucleotid contradiction; item 1 (the three data files) and item 16
(numeric message types - now a recommendation) stay open. The live list of open questions, with spec-11's answers and its own new gaps, is
`behavior-specs-10-questions.md`.

### Answered by spec-10 (from the v9 "still missing" list)

| v9 item | Answer |
|---|---|
| 1. Contradictory paragraphs | 4-level window retracted; Load Optimal is fuel-only; overgating bullets marked superseded; AI §3/§4/§9 are explicit "do not implement"; the superseded fleet table is deleted. |
| 2. AR with no starbase / Starter Colony / five AR bits | Zero capacity is a real divide fault - treat as over capacity (matches the code); Starter Colony has no components; the five bits are the planetary-scanner field set to "none". |
| 3. Concentration storage | The 100-299 roll never existed: home worlds 30-119 + at most 38, never above 157; nothing exceeds 200. |
| 4. Mystery Trader | The three gift designs, the message-78 withdrawal, skill tiers (0 Easy ... 3 Expert) and the tech cap (always 26) are given. |
| 5. Score units / excluded fleets | ceil(colonists / 100,000); removed fleets and starbases. |
| 6. Combat gaps | Missile allotment rounding, strict `<` hit test, initiative byte, whole-token target cost, damage-word meaning all given. |
| 7. Fleet/cargo | Stargate dump destination, AR unload (refused at the handler, 86), unowned planet (85), minefield unit (per ly) and type table, overgating quirk (recommend the code's choice). |
| 8. Production | Alchemy shortfall conversion and the position rule; defence cap uses the integer evaluator. |
| 9. Race traits | Flag 0x80 = failed legality check; AR mining floor and fixed 10; AR loss every moving year, Stargate excluded; Speed Trap 20 = SD or IS; BET suppress flag; LSP before Accelerated. |
| 10. Research | Miniaturization applies to hulls; the four tech-distance variants are one status line. |
| 11. AI | Units, minefield test, efficient warp, wormhole scoring, templates and cross-turn state all given (§13-§17). |
| 12. UI/messages | Field table, sibling groups, name roster use given (the data files themselves are not in the repo). |
| 13/14. Mine laying / bombardment | Exact rules given. |

### Still missing or contradictory (new)

1. **Data files cited but absent from the repo:** `extracted-game-data/default-names.txt`, `message-strings.txt`, `dynamic-strings.txt` (needed for the 999-name roster, exact message text and the engine footnote strings 41-45/396/933/847-851).
2. **Ordinary-planet surface minerals:** new-game §3 says only planet 0 gets starting surface minerals; the old paragraph says every planet does. Which governs? The code gives every planet tonnage.
3. **Radiation raise wording:** "a planet at 99 or more gets nothing" - radiation >= 99 or concentration >= 99?
4. **IFE +1 Propulsion** is "skipped when one game flag bit is set" - which bit?
5. **BET doubling for Terraforming and planetary scanners/defences** reads an uninitialised value in the original; the spec suggests exempting them - confirm.
6. **Second home planet's own minerals and concentrations** (only its population split is given).
7. **Ship designs and cost freezing:** Nova reprices designs live; the spec fixes stored costs at the start of each generation (tech gained in steps 12c/12e lowers prices only next generation). Is the live behaviour acceptable?
8. **Waypoint insertion:** the original inserts AFTER the current waypoint (Shift+left-click, 20-px snap); the port deliberately inserts in FRONT. Keep the port's choice? (A decision for you.)
9. **Move-Up/Down:** client-interface still says keyboard shortcuts reorder waypoints but none exist.
10. **Which neighbour stays selected** after a waypoint delete is unspecified.
11. **What a planet route (Ctrl+click rally) actually does** for built ships is split across files; the client-interface file only says it sets the destination.
12. **Minefield details:** how per-stack "shields absorb up to half" is computed, how the stop point maps onto Nova's continuous movement and fuel refund, whether "no departure gate: carry Jump Gates" means every design or any ship.
13. **Detonation:** the flag setter (UI/order) and the 25-point decay add-on live in other files.
14. **Bombardment stochastic rounding:** the factory/defence sum can exceed D by up to 2 - replicate or round differently?
15. **AI:** the per-race resource cut `FUN_1090_355c` (trait value and gating flag unidentified); the shared packet advisor's distance table "by driver rating"; personality 0/1 planet-pass build rules are "not itemised"; stale-design thresholds only for personality 4; §7 is still unreconciled legacy text; §2's "random exploratory colonization" and §1's "refresh threats" survive; §17 gives no slot-free equivalent for starbase slots D/H; galaxy-size index and which skill/archetype Nova's single AI represents are decisions.
16. **Message types:** Nova's `Message.Type` is a free-form string - the sibling groups need a decision on numeric types.

### Decisions for you (not spec gaps)

- **Mini Morph / Genesis Device battle grants** are still granted from battle (the spec says only the Mystery Trader reaches them) - now that the Trader's gift designs are fully specified, building the Trader is feasible.
- **Waypoint insertion order** and the desktop menu/accelerator set (items 8 and above).
- **AI scope:** the AI rewrite (advisors, role tags, templates) is large; which parts first?

## What's next

Suggested order for a spec-11 implementation pass (implement, test, verify the test fails against the old behaviour), drawn from "Implementable now, ranked":

1. **Engine corrections with a test already asserting the wrong thing** (small, high leverage): the gatling sweep rate; the minefield routine (equal-to-armor, detonation exemption / reveal / wreckage); the ram-scoop gate and minimum; theft of colonists; the research PRT exclusion and the level-26 rule; the wreckage grace mark; the packet merge test; own-packet visibility. **DONE (2026-10-04)**, except the minefield row's ram-scoop-fuel-gain-skip and message-recipient swap.
2. **New-game generation** (changes every game's opening): 0 surface minerals on ordinary planets; the star-count density formula; the 12-ly sweep; the clumping pass; the Simplified table and written defaults; then the second planet and Starting Distance placement. **DONE in the tree already (verified 2026-10-04)** for the surface minerals, star count, 12-ly sweep, clumping, Simplified table/defaults and second planet; only the home-world Starting Distance placement details remain to re-check.
3. **Scanning and wormholes**: the known / located masks and the r/4 rules, then the wormhole lifecycle.
4. **Combat and damage**: the exact movement order; the authoritative damage word with its merge / split / join formulas (which also unlocks Transfer Fleet and the merge redistribution); the salvage dispatcher.
5. **Turn order and the Trader**: pair 8, Trader visibility, message 78, the mode-0 order.
6. **Production and victory**: templates (slot 0), the catalog 10l, Packet Physics terraforming, allocation limits; victory met bits / winner mark / two races, score visibility, the Score window with Switch.
7. **AI**: the packet advisors and the category 5 fixes first (contained), then the research share, the P5 / P0 / P1 passes, the builder corrections, and - as the large items - the starbase designer with role tags, rendezvous, and the coarse-defence source.
8. **Client**: `MapViewOptions` and the planet views; name builders and mode 7; ~~battle-plan delete~~ **DONE (2026-10-04)**; rename; Find; the Technology Browser; the Goto two-press; then the summary pane, status strip and popups; the Route feature end to end once the engine side lands.
9. **Decisions needed from you**: waypoint insertion after-the-current (the spec team now recommends against the port's choice) and the delete fallback; names/badges off by default (0x00E0); whether P2 scraps the starting Santa Maria (literal) or exempts it; where the Random race roll runs (design time vs universe creation); keeping the Field/Transit minefield selector, waypoint Move Up/Down and "Append" as disclosed port additions; the report export shape (you said skip).

Pick a lane, or tell me which spec gaps you want to close first.
